## 14. Wave 14 rollback narration

PREV2, implemented by Codex on 2026-10-16, amends §§12.2 and 13. This is
operator narration only. Admission, retention, adoption, rule evaluation and
sync semantics do not change.

A probe population no longer imports every column domain of its table. Its
dependencies include the selected key/index columns, filter/equality columns,
projected or otherwise read fields, the read rule, auth and the transitive
visibility/population metadata already named in §13. A scan or point-presence
test does not by itself consult every returned row field: identity, presence and
cardinality consult population/key metadata, while scalar field access and
filters consult the named field domains; a whole-row value comparison consults
all of that row's fields. Aggregate value projections and
ordering/group keys likewise consult their explicitly named field metadata.
Forward widening of a consulted domain is monotone for the existing guard and
is not dependency uncertainty only when absent stored cells keep the same
interpretation; required-to-optional changes remain dependencies because a
literal default can become null. Selecting
the narrower release keeps §11's exact stored-value warning and §13's snapshot
population/safety warning; these count actual incompatible images and do not
recreate a table-wide guard warning. Defaults, field authority, maintained
inputs, removed consulted fields and non-widening domain changes remain
dependencies.

Leading unconditional requirements compare as their ordered conjunction. Thus
`require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` is a proved
tightening when A's first-failure region still returns the same code/message
and both predicates' inputs are supported and stable. Inserting, reordering or
changing an old refusal contract remains unproved. Displayed table rules and
requirements preserve Boolean precedence with parentheses, independently of
the implication proof.

Rollback resolves and prints its selected immutable release before preview or
live activation; JSON keeps stdout machine-readable and sends that line to
stderr. The exact private-snapshot preview then says what users of the selected
release get back. A proved loosening identifies a lost current safety rule or
mutation guard, while a read-rule loosening identifies lost current
confidentiality. Query/read dependencies whose active table population changes
show before and after table counts, and changed cap constraints show current
and target quotas. These remain table-population and declared-cap facts, never
invented per-viewer results or predicate matches. Each warning recommends
preserving the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release (or
pausing the workflow) before activation. Existing dormant row/cell/index
inventory and exact incompatible-value warnings remain authoritative.

Acceptance covers an unread enum widening with zero grouped guard warnings,
the populated rollback warning for that same widened value, an added trailing
requirement with preserved and changed refusal contracts, Boolean grouping,
rollback loss of safety and confidentiality, changed population and quota
narration with bridge advice, and resolved-target ordering before preview and
activation.

DAY4 final delta review: [Muse READY](reviews/3000.000.000-day4-r5.muse.md) and [fresh Codex READY](reviews/3000.000.000-day4-r5.codex.md), both on the same immutable revision; earlier rounds and exact source/transcript provenance are preserved alongside those records.
# LLP 3000.011.003.000.000: Packaged application proof kit

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 proof tooling, starters, fixtures, release source selection
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever
**Date:** 2026-10-14
**Revised:** 2026-10-17 (KIT3 proof and clock contracts); 2026-10-16 (REG missing frontend export diagnostic; §11: wave-14 DAY4); 2026-10-16 (§9: wave-13 DAY3; §10: wave-13 KIT2); 2026-10-16 (§8: explicit platform boundary and client-source guard); 2026-10-15 (§8: wave-12 KIT and observational listener qualification)
**Related:** [Starters](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md), [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Built application proof](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md)

STAGE staging and persistent startDev defaults are amended by [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md); KIT4 owns other ongoing helper changes.

## 1. Contract

Implementer: PROOF workstream, 2026-10-14. Wave 11 fix list 1 authorizes
one published `snapback4/proof` Node module. It owns orchestration, never client
custody or backend/bundler supervision in the product. Existing `startDev(args)`
and `stopChild(child | {url, stop})` recipes remain usable as module exports.

`startDev`, `startServe(directory)`, `startStatic(directory)` and
`startVite(directory)` return `{url, pid, logs, stop}`. Options carry cwd,
environment, arguments and bounded ready/stop deadlines. Read PORT_LO/PORT_HI
at invocation: absent means an OS-assigned port; exactly one set refuses. With
both set, validate 1 <= LO <= HI <= 65535 and bind the first free port in that
inclusive range; exhaustion refuses. Qualification's occupied first port must
be skipped, never adopted. The existing native SNAPBACK4_READY line already names its direct child PID (LLP 3000.011.004.000); no new readiness log is needed. The listening process binds first and keeps that socket;
never adopt an already-serving port. Vite uses its supported JavaScript server
API in a directly spawned Node child, strict ports, the project's config and
a per-launch identity middleware. The directly spawned frontend calls its
server listen API for each candidate; no probe-and-release step exists. With
no range it calls listen on port 0, which the tested Vite API supports with
strictPort; it reads the actual retained socket address. Static hosting uses a directly spawned Node
child with traversal protection, correct wasm MIME and SPA fallback.

All child stdout/stderr go to separate files beneath `logs/`. Read complete
lines from those files, strip ANSI, bound readiness, and reject premature exit.
Native ready lines must name the directly spawned PID. To assert HTTP identity,
the kit sets a random SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID only in the child environment;
`/auth/config` includes `proof_id` only when this variable is nonempty. It
confers no authority or bypass, and ordinary servers omit it. Both static and Vite frontend child
responses carry an `x-snapback4-proof` marker. Identity validation
precedes returning a handle. No public development door is enabled in serve.

Register every spawned child and browser process for an exit-time SIGKILL
sweep, using retained native ChildProcess handles, not process-name kills.
TERM followed by bounded KILL/reap stops a child. Every cleanup action has
its own bound and later actions still run when an earlier action fails.
`cli(args)` runs the native binary, awaits close, returns separate stdout and
stderr, and has a command deadline. It never runs npm/npx or shell strings.

`withBrowser({executablePath: process.env.CHROME}, callback)` launches Chromium
through playwright-core, closes with a bound, and registers its actual browser
process. Root needs --no-sandbox; other hosts first attempt a sandboxed launch
and retry without it only on recognized sandbox-unavailable diagnostics: "No usable sandbox", "Running as
root without --no-sandbox", "Failed to move to new namespace", "SUID sandbox",
"sandbox ... Operation not permitted", or "Failed to unshare ... Operation not
permitted". Unrelated failures never retry.
`waitForFresh` polls for nonempty changed text; `fillRN` clears and types real
keystrokes; `statusText` uses text selectors; `selectByValue` selects stable
option values. The browser executable is CHROME (when provided), undefined executablePath is omitted from launch options, then Playwright's
installed Chromium is used; an absent executable refuses at launch, with its path.
waitForFresh defaults to 30 seconds. Binary resolution is resolveBinary from
the same installed snapback4 package (override SNAPBACK4_BIN only for explicit
workspace tests); the opt-in identity requires CLI/client 0.4.28 or newer and
an older CLI omits proof_id and therefore fails the exact marker assertion; no inferred version comparison or success claim is made. cli(args) does not
invoke package managers; publication uses its existing guarded command runner.

None interprets an HTTP acknowledgement as rendered settlement.

## 2. Explicit source and aliases

`deploy dir --schema file` compiles exactly that saved Quarry file and stages
those same bytes as canonical snapback/schema.q, preserving operation names
regardless of the saved file's name. Compile diagnostics name the selected input.
This happens without swapping the working schema or generating its types.
It does not concatenate other snapback/*.q files. It allocates ordinary numeric
immutable releases, uses the same .pending-adoption marker, transaction/history,
latest selection, restoration-intent preview and no-op checks as normal deploy;
no alternate activation path or restoration bypass exists. The normal deployment path
retains multi-file behavior and activation semantics. Server/native companion source comes from the current project, while all
schema-dependent generated signatures and native builds run against the selected
schema in a private snapshot. A uniquely created snapshot project has
snapback/schema.q, captured companion source, fresh snapback/generated/
and copied native sources (target caches excluded). Host-installed node_modules
is linked into the private project; package.json preserves host module policy.
The snapshot is RAII-owned and removed on success or error; nothing is executed
through the working generated types. Its server entry is typechecked against
the fresh generated server signature before activation; its native crate builds
against fresh generated native.rs into its own native-target directory. Never reuse the working generated signature or
write selected-schema types there. Package prepared signatures and native bytes
with the captured saved source; retain the original host package installation
for dependency resolution. Standalone means no Project::locate result from the current directory/ancestors; that saved source has no companion app. Native snapshot manifests preserve their source layout and rebase only Cargo
dependency paths against the original native crate's directory. TOML parsing
covers normal, build/dev, target-specific, patch/replace and local workspace
dependency maps. Absolute paths stay equivalent; missing dependencies refuse
before compilation. Never rewrite the package's own lib/bin paths to the
working tree; those must compile against the selected private generated types.
 Missing/stale external server
import graphs fail private checking with an actionable TypeScript diagnostic. Keep saved variants outside snapback/ to avoid normal
multi-file compilation. Rollback selects an existing immutable release, never
reads the current schema. The proof `deploy` helper requires a schema path;
`rollback` requires an explicit target; `testSource` requires a source path.
`test --source file` uses the selected bytes in a memory engine (optionally
with fixtures), so it cannot adopt a working store. Diagnostics name the file.

Fixture alias arguments and assertions follow [REL3 §5](./3000.000.000.000-release-spanning-clients-and-development-contracts.rfc.md#5-fixtures-and-dev-evidence). Protocol request decoding never sees this saved-name map and accepts all ordinary literal data unchanged. Saved aliases remain explicit `$name` / `$name.field`. In saved-value argument positions (typed by fixture contracts §7), a bare string
matching an earlier save alias refuses with the alias name and `$name` rewrite
before wire decoding. Text/enum/JSON values and assertion strings stay literal. Unknown `$name` refuses by exact name lookup, never fuzzy matching.
`$name.field` requires every field in the saved object, refusing by the missing
field's name (a non-record has no fields). A `$` appearing inside ordinary text
is literal. `$$text` escapes to the literal `$text`; `{"$literal": value}`
in a fixture argument escapes any value, including an exact bare alias name. This affects only fixture case argument substitution, not protocol decoding.
Fixture preflight/sweep excludes explicitly saved arguments as before.

## 3. Starter and qualification

Web/Expo init emits frontend:proof and day2:proof (the latter takes two saved
source paths as arguments); both use the kit. The additional command(binary,
args) helper applies cli's same direct-child/log/deadline contract to external
build/test tools, without invoking a shell. Web/Expo init emits frontend:proof on the kit and declares playwright-core.
The already-shipped ?api= override is not introduced or expanded here. Proofs
use it on an owned loopback frontend and fresh browser context; production URL
policy belongs to the existing client/starter surface. The user explicitly
assigns source flags, fixture aliases, frontend proofs and their manifest entries
to PROOF; the two lane-owned text areas below remain separate.

The proof starts a memory dev and built static output, passes the actual URL
as ?api=, signs in through the generated screen (social then opens its Profile
tab, where Sign out lives) and drives a visible form when
the default todos schema is present. The fresh terminal receipt precedes the
rendered row assertion. Adapt writes missing proof files and manifest entries (no package-manager command) and keeps
application-owned proof files/scripts. At init, a complete PORT_LO/PORT_HI range is baked like --port-range;
later runtime ranges override the written defaults through launchers. Explicit
--port remains a deliberate client/interactive default. The proof kit still
allocates its own runtime URLs.

Linux publication qualification runs frontend:proof twice for every supported
frontend/template pair: web Todos, web Todos --online, Expo Todos, web chat,
Expo chat and Expo social. Every attempt uses an occupied first port and bounded
CPU load. Retain all twelve exits, log paths and runtime ranges in its receipt; any failed attempt fails qualification. Test
occupied/exhausted ranges, wrong identities, ANSI/blank ready lines, early death,
command close/drained output, browser flags, cleanup escalation, exit sweeps,
saved-source isolation and alias refusals. Three copied wave-11 proofs (018-day2-cross-country-team-splits, 020-day2-golf-tee-time-lottery and 057-service-status-page, copied from ~/sb4-loop/wave11/apps into /tmp/sb4-w11-proof-lanes) retain
their application assertions while replacing orchestration; record line counts
and execution results. Guide testing replaces orchestration recipes with the
kit and four rules: file logs; fresh signals; receipts outside unmounting
components; HTTP acknowledgement is not React settlement.

NARR owns deploy/rollback narration; DX3 owns general client recipes and discovery.

## 4. Custody scope and review disposition

The registered process set uses retained handles. Static and Vite Node hosts
start in private Unix groups and launch a live Node keeper in that same group.
The host's stdin is a driver-owned liveness pipe; driver death including SIGKILL
produces EOF and terminates the host. The keeper's stdin belongs only to the
host. Host exit (including SIGKILL)
closes it; the keeper signals its own group with SIGKILL. The driver gives the host only the read end of its liveness pipe; the keeper
likewise receives only its pipe's read end. Parent write ends are close-on-exec
and never appear in child stdio or IPC descriptors; compiler stdin is its own
pipe, never these write ends. Thus descendant inheritance cannot retain a
liveness writer after its owner dies. The keeper's live membership
pins the group identity, so no observer signals a recycled negative PID.
Vite closes its compiler before normal host exit; the keeper sweeps anything
remaining after either normal or forced exit. On Windows, direct-child cleanup
remains supported; private Unix-group custody is not claimed. Browser processes
use Playwright's process ownership and close before forced stop. Native servers use their existing SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID parent-death contract
with the proof driver's PID and already own worker cleanup. Arbitrary detached application processes are outside
this module's supported custody. Publication's existing live-keeper groups remain
the outer command owner and enforce drained groups after each proof, including
abrupt driver death. No process-name or unrelated PID kills are allowed.

## 5. Exact helper defaults

Each launch uses an independent crypto.randomUUID marker. Native children receive
that marker as SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID; the parent requires exact equality in
/auth/config.proof_id. Frontend children receive their launch UUID, and the
parent requires the exact pid:UUID header, never mere presence. Children in a
scenario need not share a marker: independent markers prove each handle's owner.
Native hosts receive --port LO-HI (or --port 0); only the child binds and prints
its actual loopback URL. The frontend child binds internally, never by a dropped
probe. Defaults: ready 30 s; TERM grace 5 s; KILL reap 5 s; CLI/command 120 s;
each cleanup 10 s; locator/navigation/fresh signal 30 s; browser/context close
5 s each. Log files have fresh UUID names below cwd/logs, never reused.

startServe serves an immutable deployed backend directory; startStatic serves
built frontend files, not a database. command uses a caller-selected executable
and argv (normal spawn PATH lookup when not absolute), shell:false semantics,
file logs and close/deadline checks; cli uses the package resolver. Use direct
tool binaries, never npm/npx to launch proof servers. Standard starter proof
reads identity/personas from its owned dev at run time, chooses guest/password/
persona sign-in and proves the visible todo add when Task exists. With a custom
Home, it proves sign-in/readiness only; the emitted instructions require the
builder's own business assertions after replacing the screen.

Fixture substitution recursively visits JSON arrays/object values. Alias lookup
is exact and case-sensitive. A $ prefix parses the name before the first dot,
then traverses each exact field; no dot returns a saved row's id. $$ strips one
leading dollar and is terminal (no re-expansion). A sole-key {"$literal":value}
is terminal and returns value untouched. Bare saved-value argument strings matching the saved
map refuse (fixture contracts §7); the wire decoder never receives or consults that map. All other
strings, including a dollar substring, stay literal. Normal Vite/compiler
children remain in the host's private group and receive separate stdin; daemonized
processes leaving it are outside custody. The keeper handles EOF with immediate
SIGKILL of its own group; the host handles driver EOF with immediate exit. Parent
write descriptors never pass to either child, so descendants cannot hold them.
Windows has direct-child stop/exit cleanup only; no abrupt-death group guarantee.

## 6. Source and discovery boundaries

Node hosts log exactly SNAPBACK4_PROOF_READY followed by JSON with kind, url
and pid; the parent reads complete ANSI-stripped lines from that launch's files,
requires the directly spawned host PID, then asserts its exact pid:UUID HTTP
header. The handle's pid is that same host PID. Vite's inline configuration
forces loopback, the selected port and strictPort:true over project settings;
the child tries each range candidate through listen, keeping the successful
socket. Selection uses {...process.env, ...options.env} at each invocation:
explicit env entries override the process environment and nothing writes back
to it. Browser undefined executablePath is omitted; a selected missing path
refuses with that path, never switches browsers; absent Playwright Chromium
also refuses. Sandbox diagnostic matching is case-insensitive on only the
listed patterns.

Save names are nonempty and contain no dot or leading dollar; ambiguous names
refuse at fixture validation before any case executes. Fixture object keys are
never substituted. $name with no field returns the
saved record's string id when present; otherwise it returns the saved scalar,
array or object unchanged. $name.field traverses object values and refuses
missing fields by name. Sole-key $literal and $$ escapes are terminal.
The CLI never writes saved variants into snapback; normal compile still reads
all snapback/*.q exactly as before. The explicit source flag selects one complete
backend even if it lives there, including the ordinary schema.q. Isolation here
means preserving working source/generated files and using private preparation,
not preventing a builder from manually placing another module in a watched
directory. Outside-snapback archive placement remains the documented recipe.

The private native tree includes its nested crates and standalone workspace.
Every copied Cargo.toml rebases dependency paths; internal dependencies point
to copied private crates, external SDK dependencies to their original installation.
Package source paths stay private. Workspace-inherited packages without a complete
standalone workspace under snapback/native refuse before compilation with the
standalone-workspace recipe. A missing member/dependency refuses normally from
the private build without affecting working files. RAII cleans snapshots on
ordinary errors/success; abrupt SIGKILL can leave a private scratch directory,
which is not a serving process or adopted release. No crash-time filesystem
cleanup claim is made.

Implemented in snapback4/proof, starter generators and explicit-source verbs.
Final independent [Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-proof-r8.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-proof-r8.codex.md) both returned READY
on the complete contract above. Earlier reviews and exact briefs remain verbatim.
The source-companion and driver-SIGKILL findings became regressions;
remaining rounds clarified existing scope and helper contracts without widening
normal source discovery or protocol decoding.

Evidence: [line counts](../snapback4/reports/20261014-proof-kit/line-counts.json),
[the four loaded-host attempts](../snapback4/reports/20261014-proof-kit/frontend-proofs.json)
and the workstream report record scoped validation, inherited root-gate
preconditions, and publication limits. This work does not publish a release.

## 8. Wave 12: honest and bounded proof kit (KIT)

Author authorization: the wave-12 KIT workstream (2026-10-14), fix list 1 and 14,
H4/H7/H8. This amendment supersedes conflicting helper/starter defaults in §§1,
3 and 5; no language, planner, protocol, authorization, sync or expiry changes.

The witness global installs on every registerWebWitness call in a browser.
Claiming/mutate sample ?witness=web at registration and retain that arm across
same-document routes; each new registration samples its URL again. Disabled calls throw E_WITNESS_DISABLED
with the URL repair. beginForm(name, expected | null, button) admits the actual
trusted click and the synchronous submit it causes, keyed to that submitter.
A later task, another submitter, or script click cannot borrow provenance.
The first unclaimable durable mutation resolves failed/E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED with
an event, operation or canonical-argument reason; it still executes normally.
Expected arguments compare exact canonical JSON including explicit nulls; null
skips comparison. Sent settlement still observes the SDK write id, outside UI
lifetime. A form with no write remains subject to the caller's bounded deadline.
Rearming cancels the old observer. The testing card carries the 15-line contract.

withBrowser launches over Playwright's pipe, opening no TCP listener. Accept the
caller's chromium BrowserType so its matchers receive its own objects. Use an
optional playwright-core peer (>=1.55.1 <2), required for browser helpers, with a
pinned development version. Playwright owns browser process/parent-exit cleanup;
context and browser closes remain bounded. Static exports validate their index
before starting a keeper, so missing-export diagnostics survive group cleanup.
Native/frontend retained-child
custody is unchanged. cli dispatches through the installed JS wrapper for every
published verb. No npm/npx/shell runner is introduced.

renderedText excludes inputs/textareas and accepts a container. waitForFresh
accepts a container while retaining its existing locator/timeout signature.
frontPage activates the page before screenshots. logsDir defaults to an OS
scratch directory outside Exact app/; explicit artifact paths are honored.
The guide adds container scope, disjoint status/row text, no input-value evidence,
and persona-roster seeding with /dev/reset {seed}, plus frontPage guidance.

Every generated frontend proof must observe a fresh terminal durable write and
rendered output. A custom Home without a writer fails with exactly
"no write scenario: replace the Home assertions"; adaptation prints that repair.
Published templates get a concrete write scenario. day2:proof passes
versions/v1 versions/v2; init stores complete current-schema copies outside
snapback/. They are starting snapshots for the builder's real next release.

Single-port inclusive ranges remain valid for schema-only init and individual
proof hosts. UI init needs at least two runtime ports and refuses a single-port
range before writing, because its backend and frontend run together.
PORT_LO/PORT_HI at init are baked into defaults, after explicit port/range flags;
at each launch/config load a current pair overrides those defaults. init reports
exactly the URLs written, even when explicit flags override its environment. Vite and
client environment fallback agree with launchers; --adapt --apply rewrites only
recognized generated legacy literal scripts/configs. Explicit --port defaults
remain available when no runtime range exists. Existing package name/version
remain unchanged. Dependency installation stays explicit: init ends in one bold
npm install line. Bare --adapt (and --dry-run) writes nothing, including proof
files, README and package metadata, then ends with the exact shell-quoted
--apply command. Only --apply mutates recognized files.

Linux packed-starter qualification adds a custom-schema negative proof, a listener audit throughout
every browser proof (proof-owned TCP listeners must stay in PORT_LO..PORT_HI),
and a real form onSubmit write. Read-only listener inspection is scoped to the
proof process tree, never other shared-host lanes. On Linux the audit uses
`strace -f -q -z -yy -e trace=listen`: every successfully returned TCP listen observed by the proof-scoped tracer must resolve its
local port inside the range, including resumed syscalls; unresolved or incomplete
traces refuse. This avoids /proc/fd permission gaps in short-lived Chromium
sandbox helpers without skipping processes or sampling away short listeners.
strace 6.8+ is a qualification-host tool, never a client dependency. The host
preflight refuses older/missing tools before builds; receipts record its version.
Process exits remain visible (-q); the successful-only filter (-z) selects
completed calls. A precisely matched `???(` fragment with no return can occur
when the live keeper is killed. It is accepted only after read-only /proc
corroboration that its process is absent or a zombie, and is retained in the
receipt's nonReturned diagnostics. A live/uninspectable process, any unknown
returned syscall, an unresolved successful listen or an incomplete named listen
refuses. No process is signalled by this audit. This is observation of successful
listens during the proof, with the decoder's non-returned exit records explicit.
Darwin consumer qualification
still runs the same write/negative cases and explicitly records the listener
audit as not-run; it cannot provide Linux listener evidence. The repository's
`listenerSnapshot` also requires Linux explicitly; its browser assertion skips
with a reason on Darwin while caller-Playwright and rendered-page assertions
still execute. It is not imported or packed by `snapback4/proof`. Synthetic trace
decoding remains portable: non-returned records use `/proc` to recognize Linux
zombies, and a read-only signal-0 existence probe elsewhere; live or
uninspectable PIDs refuse. Non-Linux zombies conservatively remain live.
The client pool statically checks direct Linux APIs and static named/namespace `.mjs`
helper calls for scoped gates (including `/proc`, `strace`, `ss` and affinity
commands). This is a common-case guard, not arbitrary JavaScript analysis;
dynamic/generated paths still need review and affected Mac execution. Retain baseline 0.4.28 reds
and candidate greens separately; no complete publication or npm claim follows
from consumer-only qualification. SURF owns general help generation; REL3 owns
fixture/dev-door testing text; RULES2 owns hidden-row testing text.

## 9. Wave 13: saved-version proofs (DAY3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. This amends §§2–3 and §8 source/fixture
selection, preserving release activation, authority, retention and wire semantics.
The shared layout is versions/v1/schema.q, original-name .q modules beside it,
and versions/v1/fixtures/. --schema and --source accept that directory; a
single complete .q still uses the canonical schema namespace. Compatibility
configuration resolves from the original project, including private builds
([REL3 §3](./3000.000.000.000-release-spanning-clients-and-development-contracts.rfc.md#3-generated-compatibility)).

Absent --fixture, --source selects saved-directory fixtures, or sibling
<stem>-fixtures for legacy complete saved files. Without those it validates
only working row files whose table exists, naming every excluded table file;
it skips the entire working cases file rather than silently reinterpreting
later-release cases. Shared-table rows still refuse unknown columns. Explicit
--fixture with --source is always strict, including unknown tables/operations. No fixtures
means an announced query-only sweep. This changes test selection, not fixture
admission, and testSource delegates exactly to this CLI path.

Init writes version directories and fixtures directories, preserving original
module names. Adapt never fills an existing saved directory from current working
fixtures; legacy complete v1.q/v2.q retain their saved bytes when a directory
layout is added. the npm day2:proof
script passes both version paths. A marked business assertion fails until
replaced; it is invoked at v1, v2, rollback and forward, before any success
claim. Frontend proofs run their frontend's own build via command() before
starting hosts; a missing/stale dist cannot pass on cached bytes. check calls
the adaptation plan and names the inherited no-arguments script with preview
and apply commands. Preview labels additions/replacements accurately and shows
changed npm script values. Exact historical default/chat/social proof variants are recognized and retain
their template assertions while acquiring the build step. Modified application
files remain owned by the app.
Guards report the actual received value.

Init bakes a complete environment range after explicit --port/--web-port and
--port-range, matching every printed URL to client, README and launcher defaults.
Runtime pairs override those defaults only through npm launchers/configs;
bare dev retains its documented default unless --port is explicit. Adapt reads
managed launchers/scripts rather than inferring ports from application client
source. The Expo setup runs init before installing the adapter, so the exact
Expo dependency set exists before peer resolution. Zero-sync Expo clients use
the online tier on all platforms, without native/web device imports.

Verification: saved directory and complete-file fixture cases, strict override
negative controls, inherited-script detection/preview/application, placeholder
failure, clean-dist build, explicit-port/environment precedence, zero-sync
Expo identity and persona clients, and packed compatibility day-two roundtrip.
The packed Expo case asserts every generated port against printed URLs.

### DAY3 amendment review record — 2026-10-16

Fresh [Codex r1](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day3-r1.sol.md) and
[Codex r2](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day3-r2.sol.md) were NOT READY.
Saved directories are now immutable through adaptation/re-init, every baseline
failure names its original resolution context, explicit saved fixtures never
skip the starter sample, and historical chat/social proofs retain their
assertions while gaining their build step. Physical read-through root retention
is preflighted before recovery writes. Each has a focused regression.

Fresh [Codex r3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day3-r3.sol.md) and
[Muse r3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day3-r3.muse.md) are both READY.
[Provenance](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day3-r3.provenance.json) records
commands, hashes, blind-session isolation and Muse's disclosed comparison limit:
it inspected amendment text against code but reported not locating the base
ref. No base-diff review is claimed for Muse. Earlier Muse attempts returned no
usable review; the structured retry produced the saved verbatim artifact.
Readiness records the review outcome, not author acceptance of this RFC.

## 10. Wave 13: durable evidence and bounded empty clicks (KIT2)

Implementer: KIT2, 2026-10-16, authorized by fix list #3 and H9.
This amendment replaces conflicting §8 defaults; it changes no wire, language,
authorization, adoption, sync, identifier or expiry semantics.

- `renderedText(page,text,{exact=true,within?})` uses Playwright's text matching,
  excludes input/textarea, and supports substring strings and RegExp rows.
  RN-web split nodes require deliberate container scope or `.first()` when
  they represent the same row. `waitForFresh` permits an omitted container;
  its bounded failure includes locator, last count/text and absent/unchanged.
- Logs default to `<cwd>/logs`: H6 deletes the harness TMPDIR, so printing a
  temporary path would not preserve failed evidence. Exact app/ callers whose
  source discovery includes logs choose an explicit artifact directory outside
  that source root. Fresh private files and direct-child custody are unchanged.
  `cli` detects the token verb and captures stdout in memory; `secret:true`
  does the same for cli/command. Secret stdout never touches disk, including
  partial/failed output. A command closes its private secret-stdout read end on
  success/failure so an exited tool's inherited writer cannot pin the driver
  past its deadline; arbitrary descendant process cleanup is still not claimed.
  Stderr remains diagnostic evidence: tools must keep
  secrets off stderr. API results still return stdout to the caller.
- `startDev` appends --memory even with custom arguments, unless --store
  (including --store=...) or --no-memory is explicit. Native store policy is
  unchanged. --no-memory is consumed by the kit to select native dev's default
  persistent store; conflicting --memory refuses. --store, if supplied, remains
  a native flag and its native validation applies (current dev refuses it; use
  --no-memory for cwd/.snapback4/store.sqlite). Browser contexts created by
  `withBrowser` add witness=web to each top-level HTTP(S) document with an init
  script before application scripts, including redirect destinations; no
  production browser or bare Playwright context changes.
- `beginForm` retains trusted click/synchronous-submit provenance and exact
  canonical arguments. An arm with no durable invocation settles failed with
  E_WITNESS_NO_WRITE and message "armed click produced no durable mutation
  (form handler returned early, or the app was not ready)": 1 second after the
  trusted click, immediately on re-arm, or 30 seconds after an abandoned arm.
  A first durable invocation with wrong provenance/operation/arguments remains
  failed/E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED and still runs normally. A claimed pending write
  has a separate 30-second proof deadline starting at claim (E_WITNESS_SENT);
  arming time never consumes settlement allowance. This is no server refusal.
  Re-arm settles a claimed unsettled observation E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED, releases
  timers/wrappers/listeners, and never cancels or retries the actual write.
  Four documented terminal paths are sent, app refusal, unclaimed invocation,
  and no invocation; pending custody itself is never proof success.
- `waitForServer(read,accepts,{timeoutMs=30000,label?})` bounds each poll and
  the whole wait, returning the accepted authoritative projection. Its deadline
  names the label and last value. It neither declares settlement nor retries
  writes; callers assert the write's sent receipt first. A read exception
  propagates rather than being mistaken for an unchanged answer.

H9 regressions load either candidate or baseline 0.4.29 modules: a row with
more text than its needle, and a trusted click with no write. Candidate must
pass and both baseline controls must fail. Affected proof/witness files run on
Linux and Darwin; no Linux-only inspection is added to the published helper.
Client readiness and account boundaries are amended in LLP 3000.013 §6.11.
The existing delivery-await census records the new proof.ts waits as proof-only
orchestration: no SDK delivery/recovery call graph or fault recipe changes.

## 11. Wave 14: truthful starter custody (DAY4)

Implementer: DAY4, 2026-10-16; authorized by fix list #10 and H9.
This amendment replaces conflicting starter defaults in §§3, 5 and 9. It
changes proof tooling and advisory output, not runtime admission or storage.

New UI starters emit the frontend proof only. `init --web --day2` and
`init --expo --day2` opt in to saved v1/v2 source directories, contemporary
fixtures and the day-two proof; `init --adapt --day2 --apply` opts an existing
UI project in. No schema or existing saved directory is overwritten.
The package's `snapback4.starter.day2` field records this choice. When the
project removes both the day-two script and its npm reference, adaptation
records false on apply; previews and check never write it. Explicit --day2
opts in again. A remaining package reference to a missing generated script is
repairable; the absence of an unreferenced scaffold is not a defect.

Starter ownership uses SHA-256 digests of complete emitted proof bytes,
retaining every shipped proof-template version and web/Expo/template variant
since the proof kit's introduction in 0.4.28. The digest census is tested
against retained source templates; a filename or bare npm command alone never
proves ownership. Proofs byte-identical to a shipped 0.4.28 or 0.4.29 day-two template are called out for
asserting no business rows. Managed bare and 0.4.29 flat-source npm commands
are rewritten only for a recognized generated proof. Custom proofs supply
their own saved paths and assertions; no missing-version or no-write claim is
made about them. The no-write/Home advice is restricted to recognized frontend
template bytes. Custom frontend scripts visibly serving dist without a build
or launching npx snapback4 dev receive explicitly heuristic advice in check
and adapt, respecting visible npm/pre-script builds. Such advice does not
claim to analyze arbitrary imported JavaScript and never authorizes rewriting.
REG owns the missing-dist runtime error in proof-host.ts; PROD owns
init --adapt --production and its production walkthrough.

When upgrading an old managed day-two proof, new saved directories capture
all current snapback/*.q modules under their original names plus contemporary
working fixtures, including test.sweep.json. Legacy versions/vN.q files are left intact and never copied
forward. Existing saved directories remain immutable evidence. The adaptation
preview identifies the current-source capture. The day-two template accepts
an optional third deployment-directory argument, refuses an existing explicit
destination, retains explicit deployments, and cleans only its own temporary
deployment. Its business-assertion placeholder still fails until replaced.
`init --expo --online` explains Expo Go and zero-sync online selection before
writing anything.

Saved-source `testSource` exposes business cases run and skipped separately
from query-sweep coverage. A run with no executed business cases refuses unless
fixtureOnly:true explicitly selects fixture/sweep validation. A nonzero CLI
failure includes the stdout tail alongside stderr/log paths. Check inspects
saved versions/vN/fixtures against that version's schema and case signatures
without executing operations or changing saved files. Validation follows seed-input
ownership: required authored fields are checked; omitted engine-assigned fields
are left to the engine and supplied engine-owned fields warn with their seed
refusal. This detects structural
row/case drift, not whether business expectations remain truthful.

Qualification runs custom hard-coded saved paths, removed scaffolding and
0.4.29 managed adaptation against the installed packed binary. Baseline
0.4.30 controls must fail those assertions. Saved-test regressions distinguish
business cases from query-sweep reads and retain failure evidence. Changed
starter commands and the explicit deployment template execute against the
packed client. Linux and Mac run affected files; no process-custody change is
introduced by this amendment. Review artifacts are linked after receipt.

DAY4 final delta review: [Muse READY](reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day4-r5.muse.md) and [fresh Codex READY](reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-day4-r5.codex.md), both on the same immutable revision; earlier rounds and exact source/transcript provenance are preserved alongside those records.

### Missing frontend export (REG)

`startStatic` checks directory existence before canonicalizing it and before
creating a keeper. A missing directory says `startStatic: <dir> does not exist;
frontend proofs build their own dist (await command("npm", ["run", "build"]))`.
The existing missing-index refusal remains. The proof owns its build. DAY4 owns
check/adapt advice for custom proofs; KIT3 owns the other helper contracts.
### Explicit upload claims and helper contracts (KIT3, 2026-10-16)

Implementer: Codex, 2026-10-16. `witnessForm(app,event,async mutate => ...)`
from snapback4/witness-web is called synchronously inside the chosen control's
handler. It accepts a DOM event or React's nativeEvent. The armed witness grants
one fresh per-arm Symbol token only if that exact native event is the active
trusted click or its synchronous submit. The supplied mutate closure carries
that token through awaited uploads. No task timing or ambient async-context
inference confers a claim. Unrelated calls to app/client.mutate cannot borrow
it; sharing the closure explicitly transfers the claim and is the caller's
responsibility. Outside a witness arm, it calls the handler with ordinary
app.mutate and has no proof behavior. The helper is not a security boundary.

The claim suspends the one-second no-write timer while the handler runs, inside
the original 30-second arm bound. Handler return/rejection without a durable
call settles no-write. Every first durable call retains operation/canonical
argument checks. A module-private claim key carries the per-arm Symbol value. Presence of a
foreign/stale token disables the active-event fallback, even during a later
trusted click. Stale tokens after re-arm cannot claim another arm; writes
still run normally and are never cancelled or retried. Claimed receipt deadlines
remain separate. Canonical argument comparison stays exact but mismatch messages
name the mismatch without serializing argument values into retained logs.

`E_WITNESS_NO_WRITE` carries `why.reason`: `not-ready` if the initial app.ready
barrier was not successfully resolved at the click, otherwise `returned-early`.
`E_WITNESS_UNCLAIMED` carries `event` for provenance/re-arm and `arguments` for
operation or canonical argument mismatch. The enum values `not-ready`, `returned-early`, `event`, `arguments` are stable; human
messages may change and are described as message changes in release notes.

`waitForFresh` returns `Promise<string>` containing fresh nonempty rendered
text. `renderedText` records a scoped substring locator for an exact string
request; if that exact locator stays absent but its substring exists, the
bounded timeout adds the RegExp / exact:false rewrite. Ambiguity still refuses
immediately and editable values never prove rendered output.
`startDev` additionally returns `now(): Promise<number>`, which fetches the
current authoritative /dev/status clock on each call with a bounded request.
Reset retains simulated time unless explicitly reset; callers must await now()
and must not cache it across reset or advance. The substring diagnostic never
changes matching or returns a substring as success; it only adds a timeout hint.
P20 missing-dist behavior belongs to REG; starter checking belongs to DAY4.

implementation at `c94694f263` received independent **READY** verdicts from
[Muse Spark 1.3](./reviews/3000.011.003.000.000-wave14-kit3-r8.muse.md) and a

and all earlier received rounds are retained with provenance. Later commits
`e0af692817` and `74ee49d4df` correct the Expo thread's proof scope, configured browser selection
in the test harness, and guide/census assertions and custom-Home qualification scope; they add no semantic contract.
//! Identity and developer inspection.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Personas

Every call carries a principal; public visitors use guest sessions; `serve` accepts bearers only. Personas are named development users; password accounts work headlessly in Node and browsers.

```quarry
use personas alice, bob
use identity password

query whoAmI():
  return viewer
```

/auth/config includes development true on dev, false on serve; hide persona controls unless devDoors is true. An email-and-password product declares `use identity password`; plain `use identity` also mints guest sessions.
`use identity guests` admits only guests; without `use identity`, both methods are disabled.

## Seed accounts headlessly

Run `snapback4 check` then `snapback4 dev` with the schema above. Save as `seed.mjs` beside `snapback/`.
Set `SEED_PASSWORD` privately and run `node seed.mjs` (Node 24). Signup returns a session; use `SEED_AUTH=login` for existing accounts (duplicate signup refuses).

```js
import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
import { createClient } from "snapback4/client";
import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
const url = process.env.SNAPBACK_URL ?? "http://127.0.0.1:4400";
const password = process.env.SEED_PASSWORD;
if (!password) throw Error("Set SEED_PASSWORD");
const mode = process.env.SEED_AUTH ?? "signup";
if (mode !== "signup" && mode !== "login") throw Error("SEED_AUTH must be signup or login");
const authenticate = mode === "signup" ? signup : login;
const result = await authenticate(url, "seed1@example.test", password);
if (!result.ok) throw Error(result.why.message);
const client = createClient({ url, session: result.session });
try {
  const me = await client.query(api.whoAmI, {});
  if ("denied" in me) throw Error(me.denied.message);
  console.log(client.viewer()); // Only the principal, never the bearer.
} finally { await client.signOut(); }
```

Use `client.mutate(api.operation, args)` for writes; require `state: "sent"` or handle `"failed"` with `why`;
`"pending"` is not success. Use one client per account. The social starter's `scripts/seed.mjs` creates N users
and profiles; replay adapters use the same path. No browser, borrowed token, persona or `/seed` is needed.
SDK calls POST `{email, password}` to `/auth/signup` or `/auth/login`: `{ok: true, session}` or `{ok: false, why}`
(wire: `{session}` or `{denied}`). Use HTTPS off loopback. There is no `snapback4 auth create`; `run --as` and `/seed` do not create password credentials.

Ordinary guest and password sessions last seven days. `refreshSession` atomically exchanges a live token;
`logoutAll` revokes every session for the principal. The client refreshes near expiry and uses an in-memory
session store by default. Both online and local clients expose `session()`, `onSession`, `refreshSession()` and
`logoutAll()`: rotation adopts the bearer before notification, and sign-out or a current-session refusal clears
it. Local `signOut()` fences late refresh responses and completes only after persisted credentials are removed.
Remote logout is best effort and does not gate that local boundary. An in-flight rotation may return its
successor for logout, but cannot adopt or persist it after sign-out. Storage failure rejects sign-out for retry;
`close()` alone preserves the session. Neither operation erases the outbox. Personas have no session; a
bare-token online client has none until refresh returns one. Only the local client has `sync()`.
`localStorageSessionStore()` is an explicit compatibility choice whose token can be read by same-origin script;
native apps should pass their secure store.

Trusted dev E_AUTH explains expiry or possible causes for unknown tokens; the SDK keeps the message.
Sign in again and discard earlier-store tokens. Serve and untrusted dev requests keep causes generic.
See `guide services` for diagnostic limits and `guide jobs` for isolated time-travel tests.

## Operations
`use personas alice, bob` declares a development cast. `snapback4 run send '{..}' --as alice` (the default
persona is alice) and the `x-snapback-persona: alice` header in development act as `dev:alice`; `persona: "alice"` on the client sends the header. A React/Expo dev UI keeps `AppProvider` and `useAuth` with
`createApp({ url, persona: "alice", device })`, one app per persona (`usePersonaApp(persona, openApp)` opens it and closes it on switch; web `?as=` and React Native pickers: "Personas in a React UI" in CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md;
`init --web`/`--expo` writes one beside a schema without `use identity`, and `init --adapt` rewrites an unmodified guest starter screen into one). No session: `app.auth` sign-in, `auth.guest()` too, refuses `E_AUTH`.
N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL: declared personas use dev:NAME. The declaration is the cast: dev refuses a persona it does not name with 401 E_AUTH, naming the line to extend, so
an acceptance test can expect an outsider to be refused (a schema without `use personas` admits no persona). Personas are a development door: the socket peer, `Host`, and optional
browser `Origin` must all be literal loopback/local http(s) values. Foreign, opaque, malformed and duplicate
Origins are refused before routing, and CORS reflects only an admitted local Origin. `snapback4 dev` is
development only: password-only apps close fixture, clock and persona doors by default;
`--unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly enables the unauthenticated local tools. Other development schemas keep
the declared cast and `POST /seed` system door. For real-user staging run `guide staging`;
deploy with `snapback4 deploy` + `snapback4 serve`. A deployed server (`snapback4 serve`) refuses personas and
seed, defaults to `127.0.0.1`, and requires `--i-understand-cleartext` for a non-loopback HTTP bind. Put a TLS
terminator in front, preserve `Authorization` and `Origin`, strip persona headers, and rate-limit there by the
actual client address; the binary trusts only its socket peer.

`GET /auth/config` is the small public advertisement for guest/password support. Catalog, schema, generated API,
native module and changes require a live principal. Signup requires at least 8 characters (Unicode scalar
values) and at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes; login accepts at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes. Password hashing runs in bounded
concurrent slots outside the engine mutex. Under `serve`, guest, login and signup share 30 attempts per socket
peer per minute, while login and signup also share 10 per normalized email per minute (E_RATE_LIMIT). Under
`dev`, loopback peers have neither minute counter; their Argon2 slots equal the host's available cores, capped
at 16 and never below 2. Startup prints the slot count and the lifted counters once. `serve` and non-loopback
peers keep two slots and the minute counters; full slots refuse with retryable E_RATE_LIMIT. Connection/worker
caps remain. Unknown emails perform the same Argon2 verification work. Retry after the minute counter or
concurrent-slot pressure clears; do not retry in a tight loop.

Use `snapback4 dev --replay` when new effects must be never delivered: writes and meters remain,
new effects are counted as suppressed, and schedule admission is held. Previously queued effects remain.
`--hold-effects` instead keeps effects claimable after restarting without the flag; `--hold-schedules`
only holds schedule admission. Both schedule holds skip missed periods; a restart without `--replay` restores normal emission for new writes and keeps the count.
The policy persists for local `run` until the next host start; suppressed effects never return.
`serve` and release commands refuse `--replay` with E_REPLAY_DEV_ONLY; production always emits normally.
See `guide jobs` for full replay semantics and the disposable-store recipe.

`snapback4 sql "SELECT * FROM messages" [--limit N] [--json]` inspects the developer's own dev store; `--release
dir` selects a deployment. It opens read-only alongside the running server and is never rule-filtered. Only
application-table views are exposed; internal tables and raw r/i are refused. Views and `data` include absent
required literal defaults; optional columns stay null. Device persistence exports retain raw storage images. The
default SELECT limit is 200, with a notice when more rows exist. `snapback4 sql '\dt'` (or `'.tables'`) lists
application tables and maintained views; `sql '\d messages'` (or `'.schema messages'`) describes Quarry
columns/nullability, indexes and unique keys. Both report the active schema generation and bounded stored row
counts: exact through 10,000, otherwise >=10,001; ephemeral counts are unavailable. `--json` returns
`generation`, `tables` and `ruleFiltered: false`. These catalog commands use the active schema, even with broken
local source. Derived sweeps use shortest text/list arguments; test.cases.jsonl supplies boundaries.

## Refusals
E_SQL_READ_ONLY means inspection received something other than one read-only `SELECT` or `WITH ... SELECT`. For
example, `snapback4 sql "DELETE FROM messages"` cannot delete rows. Use `snapback4 sql "SELECT * FROM messages"`
to inspect them, and a declared mutation for an application write. Do not combine statements or use attachment,
pragmas or side-effect functions through this inspection verb.

E_SQL_INTERNAL means inspection tried to read engine-private storage. For example, `SELECT * FROM r` or `SELECT
* FROM sqlite_master` is not an application-table view. `\dt` lists tables; `\d messages` describes one: use
`snapback4 sql '\dt'` or `snapback4 sql '\d messages'`. These views expose the developer's store, including
interpreted defaults, and are not an application authorization boundary. Use Quarry for application reads."##;

const fetchIdentity = async (url: string, timeoutMs: number) => {
  const response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs) });
  return { response, body: await response.json() as Record<string, unknown> };
};
async function nativeHost(verb: "dev" | "serve", prefix: string[], options: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost & { personas?: string[] }> {
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, id = randomUUID();
  const ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const args = [...(options.args ?? [])];
  if (!args.includes("--port")) args.push("--port", ports);
  const run = logged(resolveBinary(), [verb, ...prefix, ...args], { ...options, env: { ...env, SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID: id, SNAPBACK4_SUPERVISOR_PID: String(process.pid) } });
  try {
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_READY verb=(dev|serve) generation=\d+ url=(http:\/\/[^ ]+) pid=(\d+)(?: |$)/);
      if (!match) return;
      if (match[1] !== verb || Number(match[3]) !== run.child.pid) throw Error("proof ready identity mismatch");
      return match[2];
    }, timeout);
    const { response, body } = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/auth/config`, timeout);
    if (!response.ok || body.proof_id !== id) throw Error("proof served identity mismatch");
    let personas: string[] = [];
    if (verb === "dev" && body.devDoors !== false) {
      const status = await fetchIdentity(`${url}/dev/status`, timeout);
      if (status.body.pid !== run.child.pid) throw Error("proof dev PID mismatch");
      if (!Array.isArray(status.body.personas) || !status.body.personas.every(name => typeof name === "string")) throw Error("proof dev personas missing");
      personas = status.body.personas;
    }
    return { url, ...(verb === "dev" ? { personas } : {}), pid: run.child.pid!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
export interface DevHost extends ProofHost {
  personas: string[];
  /** Read the current authoritative development time, including all clock advances. */
  now(): Promise<number>;
}
/** The array form preserves the former startDev(args) recipe. */
export function startDev(options: ProofOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<DevHost> {
  const opts: ProofOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ProofOptions;
  const args = [...(opts.args ?? [])];
  const persistent = args.some(arg => arg === "--store" || arg.startsWith("--store=") || arg === "--no-memory");
  if (persistent && args.includes("--memory")) throw new TypeError("proof startDev: --memory conflicts with --store/--no-memory");
  // Native persistence is the default. --no-memory remains a consumed explicit alias.
  const host = nativeHost("dev", [], { ...opts, args: args.filter(arg => arg !== "--no-memory") });
  return host.then(host => ({ ...host, personas: host.personas!, now: async () => {
    const { response, body } = await fetchIdentity(`${host.url}/dev/status`, deadline(opts.timeoutMs, 10_000));
    if (!response.ok || typeof body.now !== "number") throw Error("proof dev clock unavailable; password-only dev requires explicit --unsafe-dev-doors for clock/fixture tools; use serve staging for real users");
    return body.now;
  } }));
}

  seedAccounts(options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>;
  sessions?: StagingSessions;
}
export interface ServeOptions extends ProofOptions { seedUsers?: SeedAccountsOptions }
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — operator authority stays in the existing local store proof; bulk setup refuses production.
export async function startServe(deployDir: string, options: ServeOptions | readonly string[] = {}): Promise<ServeHost> {
  if (typeof deployDir !== "string" || !deployDir.trim()) throw Object.assign(new TypeError("E_PROOF_USAGE: startServe(deployDir, options) requires a deployment directory as its first argument"), { code: "E_PROOF_USAGE" });
  const opts: ServeOptions = Array.isArray(options) ? { args: options } : options as ServeOptions;
  const directory = resolve(opts.cwd ?? ".", deployDir);
  const host = await nativeHost("serve", [directory], opts);
  const served: ServeHost = { ...host,
    operatorToken: async (service = "operator", days = 1) => {
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/.test(service) || !Number.isSafeInteger(days) || days < 1 || days > 3650) throw Error("proof operatorToken: invalid service or days (1..3650)");
      const result = await cli(["token", "--service", service, "--days", String(days), "--release", directory, "--quiet"], opts);
      if (result.code !== 0) throw Error(`proof operator token refused: ${result.stderr}`);
      const token = result.stdout.trim();
      if (!/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}$/.test(token)) throw Error("proof operator token malformed");
      return token;
    },
    seedAccounts: accountOptions => seedAccounts(served, accountOptions),
  };
  try { if (opts.seedUsers) served.sessions = await served.seedAccounts(opts.seedUsers); return served; }
  catch (error) { await served.stop(); throw error; }
}
async function frontend(kind: "static" | "vite", dir: string, options: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost> {
  const env = { ...process.env, ...options.env }, ports = portRange(env), timeout = deadline(options.timeoutMs, 30_000);
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? ".");
  const directory = resolve(cwd, dir);
  // Vite is resolved from the app, never the proof package's development dependencies.
  const vite = kind === "vite" ? createRequire(resolve(directory, "package.json")).resolve("vite") : "";
  const run = logged(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("./proof-host.js", import.meta.url)), kind, directory, ports, vite], { ...options, env, detached: process.platform !== "win32", livenessPipe: true });
  try {
    const url = await ready(run, line => {
      const match = line.match(/^SNAPBACK4_PROOF_READY (\{.*\})$/);
      if (!match) return;
      const data = JSON.parse(match[1]!);
      if (data.pid !== run.child.pid || data.kind !== kind) throw Error("proof frontend PID mismatch");
      return data.url as string;
    }, timeout);
    const response = await fetch(url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(timeout) });
    if (response.headers.get("x-snapback4-proof") !== String(run.child.pid) + ":" + run.id) throw Error("proof frontend identity mismatch");
    await response.body?.cancel();
    return { url, pid: run.child.pid!, logs: run.logs, stop: () => stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)) };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
}
export function startStatic(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("static", dir, options); }
export function startVite(dir: string, options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<ProofHost> { return frontend("vite", dir, options); }

export interface CliResult { code: number | null; signal: NodeJS.Signals | null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: ProofHost["logs"] }
/** Waits for close (stdio drained), never merges the two streams. Nonzero is returned for refusal assertions. */
export function cli(args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  return command(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(new URL("../bin/snapback4.js", import.meta.url)), ...args], { ...options, secret: options.secret || args[0] === "token" });
}
/** A bounded directly spawned build/tool command, with the same file logs. */
export async function command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}): Promise<CliResult> {
  const run = logged(binary, args, { ...options, env: { ...process.env, ...options.env } });
  try {
    await bounded(run.closed, deadline(options.timeoutMs, 120_000), "proof cli close");
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    return { code: run.child.exitCode, signal: run.child.signalCode, stdout: run.stdout(), stderr: logText(run.logs.stderr), logs: run.logs };
  } catch (error) { await stopChild(run.child, deadline(options.stopTimeoutMs, 5000)); throw error; }
  finally { run.child.stdout?.destroy(); } // Secret capture must not pin the driver after a deadline.
}
async function successful(args: string[], options: ProofOptions) {
  const result = await cli(args, options);
  if (result.code !== 0) {
    const stdout = result.stdout.trimEnd();
    const tail = stdout.length > 4000 ? `…${stdout.slice(-4000)}` : stdout;
    throw Error(`proof CLI failed (${result.code}): ${result.stderr}; stdout tail: ${tail || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stderr}`);
  }
  return result;
}
export function deploy(dir: string, schema: string, options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (typeof schema !== "string" || !schema) throw Error(`proof deploy requires an explicit saved schema file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(schema) ?? String(schema)}: did npm run day2:proof pass versions/v1?`);
  return successful(["deploy", dir, "--schema", schema, ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}
export function rollback(dir: string, to: number | "latest", options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  if (to !== "latest" && (!Number.isSafeInteger(to) || to < 1)) throw Error(`proof rollback requires an explicit immutable release target; got ${JSON.stringify(to) ?? String(to)}`);
  return successful(["rollback", dir, "--to", String(to), ...(options.args ?? [])], options);
}

export interface TestSourceResult extends CliResult { casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number }
export function testSource(source: string, options: TestSourceOptions = {}): Promise<TestSourceResult> {
  if (typeof source !== "string" || !source) throw Error(`proof testSource requires an explicit saved source file or directory; got ${JSON.stringify(source) ?? String(source)}: pass versions/v1`);
  const { fixtureOnly = false, ...proofOptions } = options;

    if (summaries.length !== 1) throw Error(`proof testSource received no unique business-case summary; stdout tail: ${result.stdout.trimEnd().slice(-4000) || "(empty)"}; logs: ${result.logs.stdout}`);

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#1 — direct children, file logs and bounded cleanup own proof orchestration.
import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
import { closeSync, mkdirSync, openSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { setTimeout as sleep } from "node:timers/promises";
import { stripVTControlCharacters } from "node:util";

const children = new Set<ChildProcess>();
process.once("exit", () => { for (const child of children) child.kill("SIGKILL"); });
export function register(child: ChildProcess): void {
  children.add(child);
  child.once("exit", () => children.delete(child));
  child.on("error", () => children.delete(child));
}
export interface ProofOptions {
  cwd?: string;
  env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv;
  args?: readonly string[];
  timeoutMs?: number;
  stopTimeoutMs?: number;
  logsDir?: string;
  /** Keep stdout in memory only (stderr remains diagnostic evidence). */
  secret?: boolean;
  /** Internal: a frontend keeper owns this private process group. */
  detached?: boolean;
  /** Internal: the frontend observes parent death through stdin EOF. */
  livenessPipe?: boolean;
}
export interface ProofHost {
  url: string;
  pid: number;
  logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string };
  stop(): Promise<void>;
}
export function deadline(ms: number | undefined, fallback: number): number {
  const value = ms ?? fallback;
  if (!Number.isFinite(value) || value < 1) throw new RangeError("proof timeout must be positive and finite");
  return value;
}
export async function bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T> {
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  try {
    return await Promise.race([work, new Promise<never>((_, reject) => {
      timer = setTimeout(() => reject(Error(`${label} timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms);
    })]);
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}
/** Attempts every cleanup, even if a preceding step fails or stalls. */
export async function cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown | Promise<unknown>) | undefined>): Promise<void> {
  const errors: unknown[] = [];
  for (const step of steps) if (step) {
    try { await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(step), 10_000, "proof cleanup"); }
    catch (error) { errors.push(error); }
  }
  if (errors.length) throw new AggregateError(errors, "proof cleanup failed");
}
export async function stopChild(child: ChildProcess | Pick<ProofHost, "url" | "stop">, graceMs = 5000): Promise<void> {
  deadline(graceMs, 5000);
  if (child && "url" in child && typeof child.url === "string" && typeof child.stop === "function") {
    await bounded(Promise.resolve().then(() => child.stop()), graceMs + 5000, "host stop"); return;
  }
  if (!child || !("kill" in child) || typeof child.kill !== "function" || typeof child.once !== "function"
      || !(child.exitCode === null || typeof child.exitCode === "number")
      || !(child.signalCode === null || typeof child.signalCode === "string")) {
    throw new TypeError("stopChild needs a ChildProcess or { url, stop }");
  }
  if (child.exitCode !== null || child.signalCode !== null) return;
  let done!: () => void;
  const exited = new Promise<void>(resolve => { done = resolve; });
  child.once("exit", done);
  child.once("error", done);
  try {
    child.kill("SIGTERM");
    try { await bounded(exited, graceMs, "SIGTERM"); }
    catch {
      child.kill("SIGKILL");
      await bounded(exited, 5000, "SIGKILL reap");
    }
  } finally { child.removeListener("exit", done); child.removeListener("error", done); }
}
export function portRange(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string {
  const low = env.PORT_LO, high = env.PORT_HI;
  if (low === undefined && high === undefined) return "0";
  if (!low || !high || !/^\d+$/.test(low) || !/^\d+$/.test(high)
      || Number(low) < 1 || Number(high) > 65535 || Number(low) > Number(high)) {
    throw Error("PORT_LO and PORT_HI must both specify an inclusive range, 1 <= low <= high <= 65535");
  }
  return `${Number(low)}-${Number(high)}`;
}
/** App-local evidence survives harness cleanup of its temporary root. */
export function logsDir(options: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" | "logsDir"> = {}): string {
  return resolve(options.cwd ?? ".", options.logsDir ?? "logs");
}
export function logged(command: string, args: readonly string[], options: ProofOptions = {}) {
  const cwd = resolve(options.cwd ?? "."), id = randomUUID();
  const directory = logsDir(options); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  const logs = { stdout: join(directory, `${id}.stdout.log`), stderr: join(directory, `${id}.stderr.log`) };
  if (options.secret) writeFileSync(logs.stdout, "stdout withheld: secret command (returned only to caller)\n", { flag: "wx", mode: 0o600 });
  const out = options.secret ? undefined : openSync(logs.stdout, "wx", 0o600), err = openSync(logs.stderr, "wx", 0o600);
  let child: ChildProcess;
  try { child = spawn(command, [...args], { cwd, detached: options.detached ?? false, env: { ...(options.env ?? process.env), SNAPBACK4_PROOF_LAUNCH: id }, stdio: [options.livenessPipe ? "pipe" : "ignore", options.secret ? "pipe" : out!, err] }); }
  finally { if (out !== undefined) closeSync(out); closeSync(err); }
  // Secret stdout never reaches a file, even briefly. Callers still receive it.
  let stdout = "";
  child.stdout?.setEncoding("utf8");
  child.stdout?.on("data", chunk => { stdout += chunk; });
  register(child);
  child.stdin?.on("error", () => {});
  let error: Error | undefined;
  child.on("error", value => { error = value; });
  const closed = new Promise<void>(resolve => child.once("close", () => resolve()));
  return { child, logs, id, closed, stdout: () => options.secret ? stdout : logText(logs.stdout), error: () => error };
}
export const logText = (path: string): string => readFileSync(path, "utf8");
export async function ready(run: ReturnType<typeof logged>, parse: (line: string) => string | undefined, timeoutMs: number): Promise<string> {
  const end = performance.now() + timeoutMs;
  let outAt = 0, errAt = 0;
  while (performance.now() < end) {
    if (run.error()) throw run.error();
    if (run.child.exitCode !== null || run.child.signalCode !== null) {
      throw Error(`proof child exited before ready (${run.child.exitCode ?? run.child.signalCode}); ${run.logs.stderr}\n${logText(run.logs.stderr).slice(-4000)}`);
    }
    for (const [path, offset, stream] of [[run.logs.stdout, outAt, "out"], [run.logs.stderr, errAt, "err"]] as const) {
      const text = logText(path);
      const complete = text.lastIndexOf("\n") + 1;
      for (const line of text.slice(offset, complete).split("\n")) {
        const url = parse(stripVTControlCharacters(line).trim());
        if (url) return url;
      }
      if (stream === "out") outAt = complete; else errAt = complete;
    }
    await sleep(25);
  }
  throw Error(`proof child ready timed out after ${timeoutMs} ms; logs: ${run.logs.stdout}, ${run.logs.stderr}`);
}

//! Immutable source releases with a database-authoritative activation pointer.
use super::*;
use crate::store_owner::Owner;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
#[path = "staging.rs"]
mod staging;
struct Stage(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Stage {fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}

fn directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    options
        .positionals
        .first()
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .ok_or_else(|| "a deployment directory is required".into())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5.1 — deployment aliases share ownership, control and worker paths.
fn canonical_directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let requested = directory(options)?;
    if !requested.exists() {
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&requested).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&dir).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(dir)
}

/// `dev` and `serve` hold the store-owner lock for their lifetime. Release
/// commands route to the admitted server when it owns this deployment.
pub(crate) fn deployment_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> {
    crate::store_owner::command(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))
}
fn current(dir: &Path, owner: &Owner) -> Result<Option<u64>, String> {
    let database = dir.join("store.sqlite");
    if database.exists() {
        let store = owner
            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if let Some(release) = store.meta("release").map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
            return release
                .parse()
                .map(Some)
                .map_err(|_| "invalid release identity in store".into());
        }
    }
    // Compatibility with deployments made before the database owned this
    // pointer. The next activation records it transactionally.
    match std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")) {
        Ok(text) => text
            .trim()
            .parse()
            .map(Some)
            .map_err(|_| "invalid current release file".into()),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
        Err(e) => Err(format!("cannot read current release: {e}")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn bound_at(root: PathBuf) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, String> {
    let project = Project { root };
    if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
        return Err("release has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release".into());
    }
    let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        return Err(bound
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .map(ToString::to_string)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            .join("\n"));
    }
    Ok(bound)
}

        for line in lines {

fn report(verb: &str, result: Result<(), String>) -> i32 {
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — release completion owns no engine/lease; bound the final diagnostic drain.
    // The background writer must get a chance to emit adoption estimates before
    // this short-lived command exits. Broken/stalled output cannot hold exit.
    let _ = snapback4_core::diagnostics::drain(std::time::Duration::from_secs(3));
    match result {
        Ok(()) => 0,
        Err(error) => {
            eprintln!("{verb}: {error}");
            1
        }
    }
}

pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    report(
        "deploy",
        (|| {
            let dir = canonical_directory(options)?;
            let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
