//! Ownership locks the database's sidecar; stat pins its inode. `serve.lock` is only an address hint.
use std::{fs::{self, File}, io::{Read, Seek, SeekFrom, Write}, path::{Path, PathBuf}, time::Duration};
use snapback4_core::{Engine, RuntimeError, Store};
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::fs::MetadataExt;

pub(crate) struct Owner {
    _sidecar: std::sync::Arc<File>,
    identity: (u64, u64),
    path: PathBuf,
    record: Option<File>,
    reclaimed: Option<String>,
    pub(crate) token_authority: Option<crate::serve::token::Authority>,
}

/// The address `doctor` dials for the server that owns this store: the
/// published loopback address itself, or, for a wildcard bind, that family's
/// loopback on the same port. A published non-loopback address is not followed.
pub(crate) fn inspector_address(store: &Path) -> Option<std::net::SocketAddr> {
    let path = store.canonicalize().ok()?;
    dialable(&address(&path)?)
}

fn dialable(published: &str) -> Option<std::net::SocketAddr> {
    use std::net::{IpAddr, Ipv4Addr, Ipv6Addr, SocketAddr};
    let address = published.strip_prefix("http://")?.parse::<SocketAddr>().ok()?;
    let ip = match address.ip() {
        ip if ip.is_loopback() => ip,
        IpAddr::V4(ip) if ip.is_unspecified() => IpAddr::V4(Ipv4Addr::LOCALHOST),
        IpAddr::V6(ip) if ip.is_unspecified() => IpAddr::V6(Ipv6Addr::LOCALHOST),
        _ => return None,
    };
    (address.port() != 0).then(|| SocketAddr::new(ip, address.port()))
}

fn resolve(store: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    match store.canonicalize() {
        Ok(path) => Ok(path),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            let parent = store.parent().filter(|p| !p.as_os_str().is_empty()).unwrap_or(Path::new("."));
            snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(parent).map_err(|e| failure(store, e))?;
            Ok(parent.canonicalize().map_err(|e| failure(store, e))?.join(store.file_name().ok_or("store path has no filename")?))
        }
        Err(e) => Err(failure(store, e)),
    }
}

fn failure(path: &Path, reason: impl std::fmt::Display) -> String {
    format!("E_STORE at {}: {reason}; restore a regular store/address file and retry", path.display())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn same_file(a: &fs::Metadata, b: &fs::Metadata) -> bool { a.dev() == b.dev() && a.ino() == b.ino() }

#[cfg(unix)]
fn regular(path: &Path, create: bool, database: &Path) -> Result<File, String> {
    snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(database, path, create, 128)
        .map_err(|e| failure(path, e))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — sidecar flocks fence backend writers and share device ownership without locking SQLite.
pub(crate) fn command(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, false, Duration::ZERO) }
pub(crate) fn device(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, true, Duration::ZERO) }
// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — a restarting host waits for watchdogs to finish group cleanup.
pub(crate) fn host(store: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> { acquire(store, false, Duration::from_secs(5)) }

#[cfg(unix)]
fn acquire(store: &Path, shared: bool, wait: Duration) -> Result<Owner, String> {
    let deadline = std::time::Instant::now() + wait;
    for _ in 0..16 {
        let path = resolve(store)?;
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(path.parent().ok_or("store path has no parent")?)
            .map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        // Stat before opening either auxiliary file; a refused probe creates
        // no database. Only the sidecar lock grants permission to create one.
        let before = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        let sidecar = snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_file(&path)
            .map_err(|e| failure(&snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&path), e))?;
        let mut cleanup = None;
        loop {
            let locked = if shared { sidecar.try_lock_shared() } else { sidecar.try_lock() };
            match locked {
                Ok(()) => break,
                Err(fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) if std::time::Instant::now() < deadline && *cleanup.get_or_insert_with(|| stopped_host(&path)) => std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(25)),
                Err(fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => return Err(busy(&path)),
                Err(fs::TryLockError::Error(error)) => return Err(failure(&path, error)),
            }
        }
        if !snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_is_named(&path, &sidecar) { continue; }
        snapback4_core::private_fs::database_file(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?;
        let database = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| failure(&path, e))?
            .ok_or_else(|| failure(&path, "database disappeared during ownership acquisition"))?;
        if before.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| !same_file(old, &database)) { continue; }
        let mut reclaimed = None;
        let record = if shared { None } else {
            let record_path = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
            if let Some(pid)=owner_pid(&path) {
                if unsafe {libc::kill(pid as libc::pid_t,0)} == -1 && std::io::Error::last_os_error().raw_os_error()==Some(libc::ESRCH) {
                    reclaimed = Some(format!("reclaimed stale serve.lock: dead PID {pid}; directory {}", path.parent().unwrap().display()));
                }
            }
            let file = regular(&record_path, true, &path)?;
            file.set_len(0).map_err(|e| failure(&record_path, e))?;
            Some(file)
        };
        return Ok(Owner { _sidecar: std::sync::Arc::new(sidecar), identity: (database.dev(), database.ino()), path, record, reclaimed, token_authority: None });
    }
    Err(failure(store, "database path kept changing during ownership acquisition"))
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
fn acquire(store: &Path, _: bool, _: Duration) -> Result<Owner, String> { Err(failure(store, "database ownership requires Unix")) }

fn stopped_host(path: &Path) -> bool {
    // A CLI owner or a host not yet announced still refuses immediately. A
    // reachable listener does too; only a prior host's cleanup gets a wait.
    // This is a refusal/wait hint, never permission to open the store: the
    // sidecar must still be acquired. Never dial a non-loopback address hint.
    address(path).is_some_and(|published| dialable(&published).is_none_or(|address|
        std::net::TcpStream::connect_timeout(&address, Duration::from_millis(50)).is_err()))
}

pub(crate) fn busy(path: &Path) -> String {
    let owner = address(path).map(|address| match owner_pid(path) { Some(pid) => format!("{address} (pid {pid})"), None => address })
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "another CLI command or a server starting up (address pending)".into());
    format!("E_STORE_BUSY at {}: this store is live; owned by {owner}; directory {}; no work was admitted. Stop its `snapback4 dev` or `snapback4 serve` process (or wait for the owning CLI command), rerun this command once, then restart the server. Exclusion holds while <database>-owner is left in place; deleting or replacing it while a process runs ends exclusion and does not stop that process", path.display(),path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(".")).display())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn address(path: &Path) -> Option<String> {
    // A missing/replaced address record cannot grant ownership. Never follow
    // a symlink (including one to the database), nor block on a FIFO.
    let record = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
    let file = regular(&record, false, path).ok()?;
    let mut text = String::new();
    file.take(1024).read_to_string(&mut text).ok()?;
    let mut lines = text.lines();
    let port = lines.next().and_then(|s| s.parse::<u16>().ok());
    lines.next().map(str::to_owned).or_else(|| port.map(|p| format!("http://127.0.0.1:{p}")))
}

/// The owning host's PID from its address record (`pid N` on the third line).
#[cfg(unix)]
fn owner_pid(path: &Path) -> Option<u32> {
    let record = path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
    let file = regular(&record, false, path).ok()?;
    let mut text = String::new();
    file.take(1024).read_to_string(&mut text).ok()?;
    text.lines().nth(2)?.strip_prefix("pid ")?.parse().ok()
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
fn owner_pid(_: &Path) -> Option<u32> { None }

#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub(crate) fn address(_: &Path) -> Option<String> { None }

impl Owner {
    pub(crate) fn report_reclaim(&self, verbose: bool) {
        if verbose && let Some(note) = &self.reclaimed { eprintln!("note: {note}"); }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#11-wave-11-factual-release-narration — unlink only our address inode; the ownership sidecar remains named.
    pub(crate) fn clear_address(&self) {
        #[cfg(unix)]
        if let Some(file) = &self.record {
            let path = self.path.with_file_name("serve.lock");
            if let (Ok(held), Ok(named)) = (file.metadata(), fs::symlink_metadata(&path))
                && same_file(&held, &named) {
                let _ = fs::remove_file(path);
            }
        }
    }
    pub(crate) fn worker_lock(&self) -> std::sync::Arc<File> { self._sidecar.clone() }

    pub(crate) fn path(&self) -> Result<&Path, RuntimeError> {
        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            let meta = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&self.path)
                .map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(failure(&self.path, e)))?;
            if meta.is_some_and(|m| (m.dev(), m.ino()) == self.identity) { return Ok(&self.path); }
        }
        Err(RuntimeError::Store(failure(&self.path, "owned database moved or was replaced; stop and reopen its current path")))
    }

    pub(crate) fn open_store(&self, timeout: Duration) -> Result<Store, RuntimeError> {
        let mut store = Store::open_with_busy_timeout(self.path()?, timeout)?;
        #[cfg(unix)]
        store.retain_ownership(self._sidecar.clone())?;
        Ok(store)
    }

    pub(crate) fn open_engine(&self, bound: &snapback4_lang::Bound, timeout: Duration, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, RuntimeError> {
        let store = self.open_store(timeout)?;
        if store.meta("release")?.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Generation("this store belongs to a deployment; use `deploy` to activate source and `serve` to run it".into()));
        }
        Engine::new_at(store, bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now)
    }
}

impl Drop for Owner { fn drop(&mut self) { self.clear_address(); } }

pub(crate) fn announce(owner: &mut Owner, address: std::net::SocketAddr) -> Result<(), String> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — live token work runs through the owner, with proof of local store access.
    owner.token_authority = Some(crate::serve::token::publish(owner.path().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?)
        .map_err(|e| failure(&owner.path, e))?);
    let file = owner.record.as_mut().ok_or("device ownership has no server address record")?;
    file.set_len(0).and_then(|_| file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    // First-line port is retained for the dev inspector; the third line names the owning process.
    write!(file, "{}\nhttp://{address}\npid {}\n", address.port(), std::process::id()).map_err(|e| e.to_string())
}

fn command_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<std::fs::File, String> {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(
            &dir.join("store.sqlite"),
            &dir.join(".release-command"),
            true,
            0,
        )
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        file.lock().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        Ok(file)
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    Err("release commands require Unix".into())
}
fn target(dir: &Path) -> Result<Option<Owner>, String> {
    match deployment_lock(dir) {
        Ok(owner) => Ok(Some(owner)),
        Err(error) if error.contains("E_STORE_BUSY") => Ok(None),
        Err(error) => Err(error),
    }
}

fn secret_path(store: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    let mut path = store.as_os_str().to_owned();

    path.into()
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — preserve the hardened auxiliary-file checks and the single owner's lifetime.
pub(crate) fn publish(store: &Path) -> io::Result<Authority> {
    #[cfg(unix)] {
        let secret = protocol::hex(&protocol::random()?);
        let mut file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(store, &secret_path(store), true, SECRET_LEN as u64)?;
        file.set_len(0)?;
        file.seek(SeekFrom::Start(0))?;
        file.write_all(secret.as_bytes())?;
        Ok(Authority::new(secret, protocol::store_identity(store)?))
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))] {
        let _ = store;
        Err(io::Error::other("local service minting requires Unix private files"))
    }
}

fn read_secret(store: &Path) -> io::Result<String> {
    #[cfg(unix)] {
        use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(store, &secret_path(store), false, SECRET_LEN as u64)?;
        if file.metadata()?.permissions().mode() & 0o777 != 0o600 {
            return Err(io::Error::other("the store token proof must have mode 0600"));
        }
        let mut secret = String::new();
        file.take((SECRET_LEN + 1) as u64).read_to_string(&mut secret)?;
        if secret.len() != SECRET_LEN || !secret.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()) {
            return Err(io::Error::other("the store token proof is incomplete"));
        }
        Ok(secret)
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))] {
        let _ = store;
        Err(io::Error::other("local service minting requires Unix private files"))
    }
}

fn refusal(code: &str, family: &str, message: &str, rewrite: &str) -> Refusal {
    Refusal::new(code, family, message).site(ENDPOINT).rewrite(rewrite).guide("services")
}

pub(super) fn denied(request: Request) {
    respond_with_cors(request, 403, json!({"denied": refusal("E_AUTH", "auth",
        "service token minting requires a loopback peer and this server's private store proof",
        "run snapback4 token --service <name> on the server host as the store owner (add --release <dir> for serve)")}), false);
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — authenticate both ends before minting; the store key never leaves its host file/memory.
pub(super) fn handle(mut request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    let local = request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|peer| peer.ip().is_loopback())
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "host"), Ok(Some(host)) if authority_host(host)
            .is_some_and(|host| host.parse::<std::net::IpAddr>().is_ok_and(|ip| ip.is_loopback())))
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "origin"), Ok(None))
        && matches!(single_header(&request, "x-snapback-store-secret"), Ok(None));
    let Some(authority) = shared.owner.as_ref().and_then(|o| o.token_authority.as_ref()).filter(|_| local) else {
        return denied(request);
    };
    if request.method() != &Method::Post || request.url() != ENDPOINT
        || request.body_length().is_some_and(|n| n > BODY_LIMIT) { return denied(request); }
    let client = match single_header(&request, CLIENT) {
        Ok(Some(value)) => match protocol::nonce(value) { Some(nonce) => nonce, None => return denied(request) },
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let (server, proof) = match (single_header(&request, SERVER), single_header(&request, PROOF)) {
        (Ok(None), Ok(None)) => (None, None),
        (Ok(Some(server)), Ok(Some(proof))) => match protocol::nonce(server) {
            Some(server) => (Some(server), Some(proof.to_owned())), None => return denied(request),
        },
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    if request.as_reader().take((BODY_LIMIT + 1) as u64).read_to_end(&mut bytes).is_err() || bytes.len() > BODY_LIMIT {
        return denied(request);
    }
    let mint: Mint = match serde_json::from_slice::<Mint>(&bytes) {
        Ok(mint) if mint.store == authority.store => mint,
        _ => return denied(request),
    };
    let binding = mint.binding();
    let Some(server) = server else {
        let Some(server) = authority.challenge(client, binding.clone(), std::time::Instant::now()) else { return denied(request); };
        return respond_with_cors(request, 200, json!({"server_nonce": protocol::hex(&server),
            "server_proof": protocol::server_proof(&authority.secret, &client, &server, &binding)}), false);
    };
    if !authority.consume(&client, &server, &binding, proof.as_deref().unwrap(), std::time::Instant::now()) {
        return denied(request);
    }
    let result = {
        // Use the same writer and identity routine as offline minting. Sample
        // wall time as the offline CLI does, independent of dev's test clock.
        let engine = producing_engine(shared);
        shared.owner.as_ref().expect("authorized owner").path().and_then(|_| {
            identity::service_session(engine.store(), &mint.service, snapback4_core::now_ms(), mint.days)
        })
    };
    let reply = match result {
        Ok(session) => json!({"session": session}),
        Err(error) => {
            let why = error.refusal().site(ENDPOINT).rewrite(if matches!(error, snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Auth(_)) {
                "use --service with 1–64 letters, digits, _ or -"
            } else { "inspect the store error, repair the store access, then rerun snapback4 token" }).guide("services");
            json!({"denied": why})
        }
    };
    let Some(sealed) = protocol::seal(&authority.secret, &client, &server, &binding, &serde_json::to_vec(&reply).unwrap()) else {
        return denied(request);
    };
    respond_with_cors(request, 200, json!({"sealed": sealed}), false);
}

fn exchange(address: std::net::SocketAddr, headers: &str, body: &str) -> io::Result<Json> {
    let mut stream = TcpStream::connect_timeout(&address, Duration::from_secs(1))?;
    stream.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(10)))?;
    stream.set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(2)))?;
    // HTTP/1.0 keeps the bounded reply length-delimited. No proxy, DNS or redirects.
    write!(stream, "POST {ENDPOINT} HTTP/1.0\r\nHost: {address}\r\n{headers}Content-Type: application/json\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n\r\n{body}", body.len())?;
    let mut response = String::new();
    stream.take(8193).read_to_string(&mut response)?;
    response.split_once("\r\n\r\n").filter(|_| response.len() <= 8192
        && (response.starts_with("HTTP/1.0 200 ") || response.starts_with("HTTP/1.1 200 ")))
        .and_then(|(_, body)| serde_json::from_str(body).ok())
        .ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("invalid local mint reply"))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — busy token commands ask the announced loopback owner, never open a second writer.
pub(crate) fn mint_live(path: &Path, name: &str, days: i64) -> Result<Session, (i32, String)> {
    let store = path.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_owned());
    let owner = crate::store_owner::address(&store).unwrap_or_else(|| "another CLI command or a server starting up (address pending)".into());
    let unavailable = |reason: &str| (2, format!("E_STORE_BUSY at {}: owned by {owner}; {reason}. Restore access to that server's loopback listener and private store proof, then rerun `snapback4 token --service <name>` (with the same --release <dir> for serve). For an older or unreachable server, stop it, rerun token offline, then restart it; for another CLI, wait for it to finish. Leave <database>-owner in place", path.display()));
    let address = crate::store_owner::inspector_address(&store).ok_or_else(|| unavailable("no published loopback listener; no mint request sent"))?;
    let secret = read_secret(&store).map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot read the 0600 store token proof; no mint request sent"))?;
    let mint = Mint { service: name.into(), days, store: protocol::store_identity(&store)
        .map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot identify the owned store; no mint request sent"))? };
    let body = serde_json::to_string(&mint).unwrap();
    if body.len() > BODY_LIMIT { return Err((1, "token: E_AUTH at --service: a service name is 1–64 letters, digits, _ or -; use --service worker".into())); }
    let binding = mint.binding();
    let client = protocol::random().map_err(|_| unavailable("cannot create a fresh challenge; no mint request sent"))?;
    let headers = format!("{CLIENT}: {}\r\n", protocol::hex(&client));
    let challenge = exchange(address, &headers, &body).map_err(|_| unavailable("server challenge unavailable; no mint proof sent"))?;
    let server = challenge["server_nonce"].as_str().and_then(protocol::nonce)
        .ok_or_else(|| unavailable("invalid server challenge; no mint proof sent"))?;
    if !challenge["server_proof"].as_str().is_some_and(|proof| protocol::verify_server(&secret, &client, &server, &binding, proof)) {
        return Err(unavailable("server proof did not verify; no mint proof sent"));
    }
    // Only a verified server receives a client proof. Neither message contains the key.
    let headers = format!("{headers}{SERVER}: {}\r\n{PROOF}: {}\r\n", protocol::hex(&server),
        protocol::client_proof(&secret, &client, &server, &binding));
    let reply = exchange(address, &headers, &body)
        .map_err(|_| unavailable("mint reply unavailable; a session may have been minted, but no token was received"))?;
    let reply: Json = reply["sealed"].as_str().and_then(|sealed| protocol::open(&secret, &client, &server, &binding, sealed))
        .and_then(|bytes| serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).ok())
        .ok_or_else(|| unavailable("server returned an invalid mint reply; a session may have been minted"))?;
    if let Some(denied) = reply.get("denied") {
        if let Ok(why) = serde_json::from_value::<Refusal>(denied.clone()) {
            return Err((1, format!("token: {} at {ENDPOINT}: {}; {}", why.code, why.message, why.rewrite.unwrap_or_else(|| "rerun snapback4 token with valid service arguments".into()))));
        }
        return Err(unavailable("server refused the local mint request; restart it with this CLI and retry"));
    }
    let session = &reply["session"];
    let principal = format!("service:{name}");
    let token = session["token"].as_str().filter(|t| t.len() == 43 && t.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"_-".contains(&c)));
    if session["principal"] != principal || session["kind"] != "service" || token.is_none() || session["expires_at"].as_i64().is_none() {
        return Err(unavailable("server returned an invalid mint reply; a session may have been minted"));
    }
    Ok(Session { scope: snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full, cap_expires_at: None, principal, kind: "service".into(), token: token.unwrap().into(), expires_at: session["expires_at"].as_i64().unwrap() })
}

                                return;

                        None => {

                if provenance { context = context.with_row_provenance(); }
                if kind != ProgramKind::Query {
                    operation_refusal(request, shared, 400, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{op} is a mutation; POST /m/{op}")));
                    return;
                }
                let retained: Option<Vec<String>> = match payload.get("searchRetained") {
                    Some(value) => match serde_json::from_value(value.clone()) {
                        Ok(ids) => Some(ids),
                        Err(_) => return operation_refusal(request, shared, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "searchRetained must be a page of IDs")),
                    },
                    None => None,
                };
                if let Some(value) = payload.get("searchRetainedOrder") {
                    let order: Vec<i64> = match value.as_array().and_then(|values| values.iter().map(snapback4_core::search_decoration::order_key).collect()) {
                        Some(order) => order,
                        None => return operation_refusal(request, shared, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "searchRetainedOrder must contain integer ordering keys")),
                    };
                    if retained.as_ref().is_none_or(|ids| ids.len() != order.len()) {
                        return operation_refusal(request, shared, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "retained search ordering keys require matching candidate IDs"));
                    }
                    context.search_retained_order = Some((order, payload.get("searchRetainedMore").and_then(Json::as_bool).unwrap_or(false)));
                }
                let settlement_started = Instant::now();
                let (outcome, cut, metadata, reads_presence, query_generation, search_current, scopes, decoration_paths) = loop {
                let reader = shared.reader();
                let evaluated = match reader {
                    Ok((epoch, mut reader)) => {
                        // Take identity from the same reader that evaluates the query,
                        // never from an engine that may have replaced it meanwhile.
                        let metadata = response_head(&mut reader);
                        #[cfg(test)]
                        test_work(&shared.query_wait);
                        let reads_presence = presence_of(&reader);
                        let context = dev_refusals::response::context(context.clone(), reader.refusal_sites.clone(), &reader);
                        let query_generation = reader.generation();
                        let decoration_paths = reader.program(&op).filter(|_| reader.schema().operations.get(&op).is_some_and(|f| f.search_decoration)).map(snapback4_core::search_decoration::paths);
                        let scopes = read_scopes::dependencies(&reader, &op, &context);
                        trace::mark("query_start");
                        let (outcome, cut, current) = reader.query_materialized_search_with_delivery_at(&op, context, retained.as_deref(), manifest);
                        trace::mark("query_end");

                        if outcome.is_ok() { lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner()); }
                        (outcome, cut, metadata, reads_presence, query_generation, current, scopes, decoration_paths)
                    },
                    // Only memory stores lack detached readers by construction.
                    Err(error) if !shared.memory_store() => {
                        return respond_timed(request, shared, 503, json!({"denied": error.refusal()}));
                    }
                    Err(_) => {
                        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
                        let metadata = response_head(&mut engine);
                        let reads_presence = presence_of(&engine);
                        let mut context = context.clone();
                        if shared.personas { context = dev_refusals::response::context(context, Arc::new(lock(&shared.bound).refusal_sites.clone()), &engine); }

                        let scopes = read_scopes::dependencies(&engine, &op, &context);
                        let (outcome, cut, current) = engine.query_materialized_search_with_delivery_at(&op, context, retained.as_deref(), manifest);
                        let scopes = read_scopes::complete(scopes, engine.schema(), outcome.as_ref().map_or(&[], |o| &o.tables));
                        (outcome, cut, metadata, reads_presence, query_generation, current, scopes, decoration_paths)
                    },
                };
                if let Some((table, id)) = evaluated.0.as_ref().ok().and_then(|outcome| outcome.pending_opening.as_ref()) {
                    if settlement_started.elapsed() >= Duration::from_secs(30) || crate::lifecycle::stopping() {
                        let mut why = Refusal::new("E_QUERY_RETRY", "client", "timed disclosure did not settle within the query wait; retry the query").guide("client");
                        why.retryable = true;
                        return respond_timed(request, shared, 503, json!({"denied":why}));
                    }
                    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
                    let now = match engine.materialize_time_audience_at(table, id, context.now.unwrap_or_else(|| shared.now())) {
                        Ok(now) => now,
                        Err(error) => return respond_timed(request, shared, 503, json!({"denied":error.refusal()})),
                    };
                    shared.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, now);
                    shared.durable_committed(&engine);
                    drop(engine);
                    if let Err(why) = session_identity::principal_of(&request, shared) {
                        return respond_timed(request, shared, 401, json!({"denied":why}));
                    }
                    std::thread::yield_now();
                    continue;
                }
                break evaluated;
                };
                let (store_id, head) = match metadata { Ok(head) => head, Err(why) => { respond_timed(request, shared, 503, json!({ "denied": why })); return; } };

    // Revalidate under the writer: neither a slow body nor an adoption queue
    // may carry fixture authority into a newly password-only schema.
    pub(crate) fn dev_authority(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
        if self.personas && (self.unsafe_dev_doors || !(engine.schema().auth.password && !engine.schema().auth.guests)) { Ok(()) }
        else { Err(dev_doors::closed()) }
    }
    pub fn now(&self) -> i64 { lock(&self.clock).now().max(self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire)) }
    pub(crate) fn refresh_time_audience_clock(&self, engine: &Engine, now: i64) {
        if engine.schema().tables.values().any(|table| table.not_before.is_some()) {
            let durable = engine.store().meta("time_audience_clock").ok().flatten()
                .and_then(|value| value.parse::<i64>().ok()).unwrap_or(i64::MIN);
            self.time_audience_clock.fetch_max(now.max(durable), Ordering::AcqRel);
        } else {
            self.time_audience_clock.store(i64::MIN, Ordering::Release);
        }
    }
    fn advance_time_audiences(&self) -> Result<i64, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        let requested = lock(&self.clock).now();
        // Non-timed reader traffic must retain its independent reader lane,
        // including while adoption or a mutation deliberately holds the writer.
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(requested); }
        let mut engine = lock(&self.engine);
        let before = engine.seq()?;
        let now = engine.advance_time_audiences_at(requested)?;
        self.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, now);
        if engine.seq()? != before { self.durable_committed(&engine); }
        Ok(now)
    }
    fn next_time_audience(&self) -> Result<Option<i64>, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        if self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire) == i64::MIN { return Ok(None); }
        lock(&self.engine).next_time_audience_at()
    }
    fn time_audience_delay(&self, at: i64) -> Duration {
        let raw = lock(&self.clock).now();
        let floor = self.time_audience_clock.load(Ordering::Acquire);
        Duration::from_millis(if at <= floor { 0 } else { at.saturating_sub(raw).max(0) }.try_into().unwrap_or(u64::MAX))
    }
    pub(crate) fn worker_owner(&self) -> Option<Arc<std::fs::File>> { self.owner.as_ref().map(|owner| owner.worker_lock()) }
}

/// A panicking request must not turn one poisoned process mutex into a
/// permanent server-wide panic loop. The guarded value is retained, the
/// poison marker is cleared, and the next request can make progress.
pub(crate) fn lock<T: Lockable>(mutex: &T) -> T::Guard<'_> {
    mutex.recover_lock()
}

    active: Mutex<BTreeSet<(String, String)>>,

    pub(crate) fn enter<'a>(&'a self, asset: &str, principal: &str) -> AssetTransfer<'a> {
        let key = (asset.to_owned(), principal.to_owned());

        while active.contains(&key) {
            active = self.available.wait(active).unwrap_or_else(|poisoned| poisoned.into_inner());
        }
        active.insert(key.clone());
        AssetTransfer { transfers: self, key }

//! HTTP envelopes are bounded bytes, not data-program IR or SQL authority.
use super::{ActionSpec, WorkSpec};
use crate::ir::{ArgType, Expr, Program, ProgramKind, Stmt};
use crate::value::{Value, decode_bytes_hex, digest};
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

pub const BODY_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;
pub const HEADER_BYTES: usize = 16 * 1024;
pub const HEADER_COUNT: usize = 64;
pub const ENVELOPE_BYTES: u32 = 256 * 1024;
pub const ACTION_PREFIX: &str = "@route/";
fn default_wait() -> u64 { 5_000 }

// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.1 — declaration publishes a route; verified admission precedes its durable handler.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RouteSpec {
    pub path: String,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub service: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub work: WorkSpec,
    #[serde(default = "default_wait")]
    pub wait_ms: u64,
}

impl RouteSpec {
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<(), String> {
        validate_path(&self.path)?;
        if !(1..=25_000).contains(&self.wait_ms) { return Err("route wait is 1ms through 25s".into()); }
        if !self.work.policy.valid() { return Err("route work policy is outside its bounds".into()); }
        if self.service.as_ref().is_some_and(|service| !service.strip_prefix("service:").is_some_and(|name|
            !name.is_empty() && name.len() <= 64 && name.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'_' || b == b'-'))) {
            return Err("a provider route names an explicit service:<name> seat".into());
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Private work is never a public action declaration or client operation.
    /// Even accidental insertion into the public program map cannot admit it.
    pub fn program(&self, name: &str) -> Program {
        Program { name: format!("{ACTION_PREFIX}{name}"), kind: ProgramKind::Action,
            args: BTreeMap::from([("request".into(), ArgType::Json { max_bytes: ENVELOPE_BYTES })]),
            read_time_granularity_ms: None, native: false, returned_shapes: Default::default(),
            body: vec![Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Literal(Value::Bool(false)), code: "E_ROUTE_PRIVATE".into(), site: None, message: None }] }
    }

    pub fn action_spec(&self) -> ActionSpec { ActionSpec { returns: None, work: self.work.clone() } }
    pub fn contract(&self, name: &str) -> String { digest(&("route:v1", name, self)) }
}

pub fn validate_path(path: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
    if path.len() > 256 || !path.starts_with('/') || path == "/" || path.ends_with('/')
        || path.split('/').skip(1).any(|part| part.is_empty() || matches!(part, "." | "..")
            || !part.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(b, b'-' | b'_' | b'.'))) {
        return Err("a route has a static absolute ASCII path without query, escapes, empty or dot segments".into());
    }
    let first = path.trim_start_matches('/').split('/').next().unwrap_or("");
    if matches!(first, "q" | "m" | "a" | "actions" | "effects" | "routes" | "assets" | "auth" | "dev" | "seed" | "sync" | "changes" | "schema" | "probe" | "api.ts" | "native.wasm") {
        return Err("this route path belongs to a core HTTP endpoint".into());
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// Header spelling is the HTTP parser's normalized spelling. Only body bytes
/// are exact. Pairs retain multiplicity, including independent Set-Cookie.
pub type Headers = Vec<(String, String)>;

fn headers_valid(headers: &Headers, response: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
    if headers.len() > HEADER_COUNT || headers.iter().map(|(k, v)| k.len().saturating_add(v.len())).sum::<usize>() > HEADER_BYTES {
        return Err("route headers exceed 64 fields or 16 KiB".into());
    }
    for (name, value) in headers {
        if name.is_empty() || !name.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"!#$%&'*+-.^_`|~".contains(&b))
            || !value.bytes().all(|b| b == b'\t' || (b' '..=b'~').contains(&b)) {
            return Err("route headers need HTTP token names and printable ASCII values without newlines".into());
        }
        if response && matches!(name.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str(), "connection" | "content-length" | "transfer-encoding" | "keep-alive" | "upgrade" | "proxy-authenticate" | "proxy-authorization" | "te" | "trailer") {
            return Err("route responses cannot supply transport-owned headers".into());
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn body_bytes(encoded: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
    if encoded.len() > BODY_BYTES * 2 { return Err("a route body exceeds 64 KiB".into()); }
    decode_bytes_hex(encoded).ok_or_else(|| "route bytes use canonical lowercase base16".into())
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase", deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct RouteRequest {
    pub method: String,
    pub path: String,
    pub headers: Headers,
    /// Same byte wire spelling as Quarry bytes; no extra codec dependency.
    pub body_hex: String,
}

impl RouteRequest {
    pub fn validate(&self, spec: &RouteSpec) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
        spec.validate()?;
        if self.method != "POST" || self.path != spec.path { return Err("this request does not match its declared POST route".into()); }
        headers_valid(&self.headers, false)?;
        body_bytes(&self.body_hex)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase", deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct RouteResponse {
    pub status: u16,
    pub headers: Headers,
    pub body_hex: String,
    /// Plain application GiveUp is recorded and acknowledged without exposing
    /// its text. Infrastructure refusals and parked steps never use this form.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub application_refusal: Option<String>,
}

impl RouteResponse {
    pub fn validate(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String> {
        if !(200..=599).contains(&self.status) { return Err("a route response status is 200 through 599".into()); }
        headers_valid(&self.headers, true)?;
        let bytes = body_bytes(&self.body_hex)?;
        if matches!(self.status, 204 | 205 | 304) && !bytes.is_empty() { return Err("this response status cannot have a body".into()); }
        if let Some(message) = &self.application_refusal {
            if self.status != 204 || !self.headers.is_empty() || !bytes.is_empty() || message.len() > 2_000 {
                return Err("an acknowledged application refusal is a bare 204 and a bounded redacted reason".into());
            }
        }
        Ok(bytes)
    }
}

        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":offset, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    }
}

/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
    let service=if service_route {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
        }
    } else {
        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
        None
    };
    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };
    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
    let program = match program {
        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
    };
    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
        for (field, value) in fields {
            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
            }
        }
    }
    let mut admitted=lock(&shared.engine);
    crate::release_requests::stamp(admitted.generation());
    if !service_route && let Err(why) = shared.dev_authority(&admitted) { drop(admitted); return refuse(request,403,why); }
    if let Some(service)=&service {
        if let Err(why)=super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&admitted,service) {drop(admitted);return refuse(request,403,why);}
    }
    let queued=admitted.enqueue_job(name,args,shared.now());
    drop(admitted);
    crate::release_requests::executed();
    let id = match queued {
        Ok(id) => id,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    };
    // FIFO: earlier queued runs finish first, as with `run` on a stopped store;
    // the background tick may run some chunks too, through the same engine.
    let run = loop {
        let current = {let engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.job(&id)};
        match current {
            Ok(Some(run)) if matches!(run.state, JobState::Done | JobState::Parked) || crate::lifecycle::stopping() => break run,
            Ok(Some(_)) => {}
            Ok(None) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&RuntimeError::Store(format!("job {id} vanished")).refusal())})),
            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
        }
        let (chunk, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| (engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget), engine.take_native_logs()));
        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
        match chunk {
            Ok(Some(chunk)) if chunk.state == JobState::Parked && shared.personas => shared.refusals.job(&chunk),
            Ok(_) => {}
            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
        }
    };
    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} {} on request: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, if run.state == JobState::Done { "done" } else { "parked" },
        run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" });
    respond(request, 200, json!({ "job": run.name, "id": run.id, "state": run.state, "chunks": run.chunks, "chunkSize": run.chunk_size, "retries": run.retries, "heapPeak": run.heap_peak, "rowsWritten": run.rows_written, "error": run.error }));
}

pub(super) fn clock_status(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    if !shared.personas { return respond(request, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::release())})); }
    let result = (|| {
        let (epoch, reader) = shared.reader()?;
        let note = crate::dev_clock_store::ahead(&reader, shared.now());
        lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
        note
    })();
    match result {
        Ok(note) => { let now = shared.now(); respond(request, 200, json!({"storeAhead":note,"now":now,"skewMs":now - snapback4_core::now_ms()})) },
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    }
}
                    Ok(generated) => generated,
                    Err(error) => {
                        snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: cannot write generated types: {error}\nsnapback4 dev: serving the previous generation");
                        continue;
                    }
                };
                if let Err(error) = project.check_server_at(&mut stamp) {
                    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {error}\nsnapback4 dev: serving the previous generation");
                    continue;
                }
                let declared = match crate::dev_worker::declared(&project) {
                    Ok(workers) => workers,
                    Err(error) => {
                        snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {error}\nsnapback4 dev: serving the previous generation");
                        continue;
                    }
                };
                if crate::dev_worker::source(&project).is_some() || !declared.is_empty() {
                    if let Err(error) = crate::dev_worker::validate_environment(&bound.schema) {
                        snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {error}\nsnapback4 dev: serving the previous generation");
                        continue;
                    }
                }
                let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
                let adopted = adopt_candidate(&shared, &mut engine, &bound, || {
                    shared.owner.as_ref().ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Store("temporary server cannot reopen a persisted store".into()))
                        .and_then(|owner| owner.open_engine(&bound, Duration::from_secs(5), shared.now()))
                });
                match adopted {
                    Ok((next, reopened)) => {
                        shared.refresh_time_audience_clock(&engine, shared.now());
                        seen_generation = next;
                        crate::lifecycle::generation(next);

                        if reopened || rebuilt.is_some() {
                            match crate::native::install(&mut engine, &project) {
                                Ok(installed) => {
                                    *lock(&shared.native) = installed;
                                    if let Some(built) = &rebuilt { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: native module rebuilt ({} B, {} ms): {}", built.bytes, built.ms, built.jobs.join(", ")); }
                                }
                                Err(problem) => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {problem}"),
                            }
                        }
                        lock(&shared.readers).replace(&engine, &bound.refusal_sites);
                        // Publish the advertised backend and identity together:
                        // /schema takes these guards in the same order.
                        let mut active = lock(&shared.bound);
                        let mut generation = lock(&shared.generation);
                        for note in &residency_notes { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("note: {note}"); }
                        for note in &bound.notes { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("note: {note}"); }
                        shared.latency.adopt(&bound);
                        *active = bound;
                        *generation = next;
                        // Claims authenticate their supervisor revision while
                        // holding this engine guard: the old child cannot
                        // claim new work during the supervisor's stop delay.
                        shared.worker_revision.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Release);
                        snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: schema adopted as generation {} ({:?}) in {} ms; types: {}{}", *generation, worst, started.elapsed().as_millis(), generated.display(), summary(&engine));
                        for step in steps {
                            snapback4_core::diagnostic!("  {:?}: {}", step.change, step.message);
                        }
                        // Adoption can itself commit a populated maintain rebuild.
                        shared.publish_engine(&engine);
                    }
                    Err(error) => {
                        retry = retryable(&error);
                    }
                }
            }
            Ok(bound) => {
                let (reports, hidden) = crate::verbs::problem_reports(&bound, true, verbose);
                let shown = reports.len();
                for report in reports { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("{}", crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, &report, verbose)); }
                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {shown} problem{} in snapback/; serving the previous generation", if shown == 1 { "" } else { "s" });
                if hidden > 0 { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: {hidden} redundant cascade diagnostic(s) hidden; use --verbose to show them"); }
            }
            Err(error) => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4 dev: cannot read snapback/: {error}"),
        }
    }
}

//! Read checkout is independent of the schema-adoption writer.
use super::*;

// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — reader reuse retains fresh transactions and retires old native modules.
/// Reuse only detached read owners. Each query still opens and rolls back
/// its own identity-checked SQLite transaction; no result or viewer is cached.
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct QueryReaders {
    pub(super) epoch: Arc<()>,
    pub(super) idle: Vec<Engine>,
    pub(super) source: Option<Engine>,
    memory: bool,
    pub(super) refusal_sites: Arc<dev_refusals::Sites>,
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) fail_open_after: Option<usize>,
    #[cfg(test)]
    pub(super) retry_open: Option<Arc<dyn Fn() + Send + Sync>>,
    candidate_queries: BTreeSet<String>,
    launching_feed: bool,
}

impl QueryReaders {
    pub(super) fn take(
        &mut self,
        engine: &Engine,
    ) -> Result<(Arc<()>, Engine), snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        self.memory = engine.store().conn().path().is_none_or(str::is_empty);
        if self.source.is_none() {
            self.source = engine.reader().ok();
        }
        let reader = match self.idle.pop() {
            Some(reader) => reader,
            None => engine.reader()?,
        };
        Ok((self.epoch.clone(), reader))
    }

    pub(super) fn put(&mut self, epoch: &Arc<()>, reader: Engine) {
        if Arc::ptr_eq(epoch, &self.epoch) && self.idle.len() < REQUEST_WORKERS {
            self.idle.push(reader);
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn invalidate(&mut self) {
        // Native code can change without a schema-generation change. Loans
        // retain their old epoch so an in-flight return cannot repopulate it.
        self.epoch = Arc::new(());
        self.idle.clear();
        self.source = None;
        self.candidate_queries.clear();
        self.launching_feed = false;
    }
}

impl QueryReaders {
    pub(super) fn new(engine: &Engine, sites: &dev_refusals::Sites) -> Self {
        Self {
            source: engine.reader().ok(),
            refusal_sites: Arc::new(sites.clone()),
            memory: engine.store().conn().path().is_none_or(str::is_empty),
            ..Self::default()
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn replace(&mut self, engine: &Engine, sites: &dev_refusals::Sites) {
        self.invalidate();
        self.refusal_sites = Arc::new(sites.clone());
        self.memory = engine.store().conn().path().is_none_or(str::is_empty);
        self.source = engine.reader().ok();
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — retry a failed factory on existing writer work; reads still refuse immediately.
    pub(super) fn retry_missing(&mut self, engine: &Engine) {
        if !self.memory && self.source.is_none() {
            self.source = latency::acquire(|| {
                #[cfg(test)]
                if let Some(open) = &self.retry_open { open(); }
                engine.reader()
            }).ok();
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn begin_adoption(&mut self, candidate: &Bound) {
        self.launching_feed = self.source.as_ref().is_some_and(|old| candidate.schema.timelines.iter()
            .any(|next| !old.schema().timelines.iter().any(|t| t.same_declaration(next))));
        self.candidate_queries = candidate
            .programs
            .iter()
            .filter(|p| p.program.kind == ProgramKind::Query)
            .map(|p| p.program.name.clone())
            .collect();
    }
    pub(super) fn finish_adoption(&mut self) {
        self.candidate_queries.clear();
        self.launching_feed = false;
    }
    pub(super) fn launching_feed(&self) -> bool { self.launching_feed }
    pub(super) fn pending(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.candidate_queries.contains(name)
    }
    fn detached(&mut self) -> Option<Result<(Arc<()>, Engine), snapback4_core::RuntimeError>> {
        #[cfg(test)]
        if let Some(remaining) = &mut self.fail_open_after {
            if *remaining == 0 {
                self.idle.clear();
                return Some(Err(snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Store("injected reader-open failure; retry the request".into())));
            }
            *remaining -= 1;
        }
        let source = self.source.as_ref()?;
        Some(match self.idle.pop() {
            Some(reader) => Ok((self.epoch.clone(), reader)),
            None => source.reader().map(|reader| (self.epoch.clone(), reader)),
        })
    }
}
impl Shared {
    // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.6 — a fresh WAL snapshot reads the old program during atomic activation.
    // The source is only a connection factory: no transaction, answer or viewer
    // is retained. Identity guards still refuse an obsolete generation.
    pub(super) fn reader(&self) -> Result<(Arc<()>, TimedReader), snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        let (result, sites) = self.acquire_reader(|| {
            let start = trace::begin();
            let mut readers = lock(&self.readers);
            trace::waited(start, "readers");
            (readers.detached(), readers.refusal_sites.clone())
        });
        if let Some(result) = result {
            return result.map(|(epoch, reader)| (epoch, TimedReader { engine: reader, refusal_sites: sites, _timing: latency::EngineSpan::new() }));
        }
        // A missing factory can also mean opening a file-backed reader failed.
        // Never acquire the writer to repair that failure under a read permit.
        Err(snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Store("detached reader unavailable; retry the request after the store is available".into()))
    }
    fn acquire_reader<T>(&self, open: impl FnOnce() -> T) -> T {
        latency::acquire(|| {
            #[cfg(test)]
            test_work(&self.reader_open);
            open()
        })
    }
    pub(super) fn open_reader(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<Engine, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        self.acquire_reader(|| engine.reader())
    }
    pub(super) fn take_reader(&self, engine: &Engine) -> Result<(Arc<()>, Engine), snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        self.acquire_reader(|| lock(&self.readers).take(engine))
    }
    pub(super) fn memory_store(&self) -> bool { lock(&self.readers).memory }

    // Identity, head and generation are captured from one reader incarnation.
    pub(super) fn read_identity(&self) -> Result<(String, u64, u64), Refusal> {
        match self.reader() {
            Ok((epoch, mut reader)) => {
                let generation = reader.generation();
                let head = response_head(&mut reader).map(|(id, head)| (id, head, generation));
                lock(&self.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
                head
            }
            Err(_) if self.memory_store() => {
                let mut engine = lock(&self.engine);
                let generation = engine.generation();
                response_head(&mut engine).map(|(id, head)| (id, head, generation))
            }
            Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn read_head(&self) -> Result<(String, u64), Refusal> {

+# Staging with real users
+
+Use `serve` for staging, shared previews and `probe:serve`. It enforces the same
+password identity and rules as production. `dev` always advertises
+`development:true` in `/auth/config`, even with closed doors; probes should
+refuse it. `guide deploy` covers release evolution; PROD2 owns starter adaptation.
+
+## Password roles and operator provisioning
+
+Save this complete source as `snapback/schema.q` in an app with snapback4 installed.
+Signup creates an account without a role. Only the local filesystem operator can
+bootstrap this roster. The insert rule enforces the boundary independently of
+the mutation. SEC owns self-promotion diagnostics and invariant proofs.
+
+```quarry
+use identity password
+table roles:
+  principal: principal
+  role: enum('admin', 'member')
+  unique principal
+  immutable principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
+  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
+  online only
+mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
+  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }
+query myRole():
+  return roles[viewer]
+table notes:
+  owner: principal
+  body: text 1..200
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+mutation add(body: text 1..200):
+  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
+query myNotes():
+  return notes where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id
+```
+
+## Stand up staging
+
+Run `npx snapback4 check` to generate the API. Save this as `staging.mjs` next to
+snapback/, then run `node staging.mjs`. Use `node staging.mjs --once` for CI setup
+and shutdown. The deployment and private credentials file remain for inspection;
+choose fresh names for each independent run. Add both to .gitignore. PORT_LO and
+PORT_HI optionally choose a port range. The printed URL belongs to `serve`.
+
+```js
+import { openSync, writeFileSync, closeSync } from "node:fs";
+import { deploy, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
+import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
+
+await deploy("staging", "snapback", { args: ["--staging"] });
+const host = await startServe("staging", { seedUsers: {
+  roles: { admin: 2, member: 2 }, provision: api.provision,
+} });
+try {
+  // This file contains real passwords and bearer tokens. Never commit it.
+  const fd = openSync("staging-users.json", "wx", 0o600);
+  try { writeFileSync(fd, JSON.stringify(host.sessions)); } finally { closeSync(fd); }
+  for (const accounts of Object.values(host.sessions)) {
+    for (const account of accounts) {
+      const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/add", {
+        method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.token}` },
+        body: JSON.stringify({ args: { body: `Welcome ${account.role}` } }),
+        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
+      });
+      if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("staging data write refused");
+    }
+  }
+  console.log(JSON.stringify({ backendUrl: host.url, credentialsFile: "staging-users.json" }));
+  if (!process.argv.includes("--once")) await new Promise(resolve => {
+    process.once("SIGTERM", resolve); process.once("SIGINT", resolve);
+  });
+} finally { await host.stop(); }
+```
+
+`host.seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` performs additional batches; an
+optional `args(account)` maps principal/role/email to your mutation's arguments.
+Each call creates new random email/password pairs and returns role arrays of
+`{role,email,password,principal,token,expiresAt}`. Ordinary sessions last seven
+days; sign in again with `login` from snapback4/auth when needed. Setup is
+sequential and bounded (default five minutes, timeoutMs overrides it, max 100
+accounts). Only explicit identity throttling refusals retry; ambiguous writes
+do not. Partial failure can leave accounts/grants: inspect or discard this
+disposable deployment before retrying. Returned credentials never enter helper
+logs. Print them only to a private operator destination, as above.
+
+## Production refuses bulk setup
+
+`deploy --staging` can classify only a new store. Existing unmarked stores are
+production, and refuse relabelling. Classification stays with the store across
+hot deploys, rollbacks and restarts; copying production into a directory named
+staging does not change it. `deploy --check --staging` does not mark a store.
+There is no promotion flag: deploy real production into its own fresh directory
+and provision only intended users. `seedAccounts` and startServe's seedUsers
+refuse production or dev before minting a token or creating any account. A
+normal production startServe still supports operatorToken for explicit grants.
+
+### E_STAGING
+
+E_STAGING refuses production relabelling or bulk account setup on a non-staging
+host. Choose a separate fresh `deploy --staging` store; use the explicit
+first-admin recipe below for intended production users. Source edits and a
+directory named staging never change a production store's classification.
+
+## First admin on production
+
+Deploy the same schema with `npx snapback4 deploy production` (omit --staging).
+Save the following as `first-admin.mjs`. Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and
+FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately, then run `node first-admin.mjs` as the store
+owner while no other serve owns its port. This script starts and stops serve;
+restart it normally afterwards. For an already running deployment use
+`snapback4 token --service operator --days 1 --release production` locally and
+the same authenticated mutation. Keep the service token out of the frontend.
+
+```js
+import { signup, login } from "snapback4/auth";
+import { startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
+const email = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL, password = process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD;
+if (!email || !password) throw Error("Set FIRST_ADMIN_EMAIL and FIRST_ADMIN_PASSWORD privately");
+const host = await startServe("production");
+try {
+  // Set FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING=1 only for an account you already created.
+  const account = await (process.env.FIRST_ADMIN_EXISTING === "1" ? login : signup)(host.url, email, password);
+  if (!account.ok) throw Error(account.why.message);
+  const token = await host.operatorToken();
+  const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/provision", {
+    method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${token}` },
+    body: JSON.stringify({ args: { principal: account.session.principal, role: "admin" } }),
+    signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
+  });
+  if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("operator grant refused");
+  console.log("First admin provisioned; sign in with the chosen email and password");
+} finally { await host.stop(); }
+```
+
+No first-user race or self-promotion mutation is needed. Subsequent grants use
+the same operator process or an explicitly authorized admin policy. Invite codes
+must be server-validated, single-use grants issued by an authorized operator;
+an arbitrary caller-supplied code or role is not authority.
+
+## Local fixture tools are explicit
+
+Password-only dev closes /seed, /dev/* and persona headers by default.
+`dev --unsafe-dev-doors` explicitly opens local fixture and clock tools and
+prints a warning; it still requires loopback Host/Origin, and personas must be
+declared with `use personas`. The local token-mint protocol keeps its existing
+private store-owner proof. Serve never opens these development tools.
+`/auth/config` reports `environment`, `development`, and `devDoors` separately.
+`startDev()` persists by default, matching native dev; pass args:["--memory"]
+for disposable storage. now() needs the explicit clock door on password-only
+dev. A proof for real users uses the serve recipe above.

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
