Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS=-USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7). A plain production Cargo build retains the dependency's ordinary SQLite policy; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling

pub enum LoadMode { Import, Restore }

/// Who runs the program, with what, and when.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Context {
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(crate) refusal_trace: Option<refusal_trace::RefusalTrace>,
    pub retained_search: bool,
    /// Bounded retained ordering keys supplied by the coordinator, never authority.
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub search_retained_order: Option<(Vec<i64>, bool)>,
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub(crate) search_retained_candidates: Vec<(String, i64, bool)>,
    pub read_spent: Option<crate::meter::Metered>,
    pub viewer: String,
    pub kind: String,
    pub args: BTreeMap<String, Value>,
    pub now: Option<i64>,
    pub read_time: Option<i64>,
    pub(crate) row_provenance: bool,
    /// Digest only: the core resolves it in the admission transaction.
    pub(crate) session_digest: Option<String>,
    /// Original HTTP arguments, before typed decoding or default insertion.
    pub wire_args: Option<serde_json::Value>,
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub delivery_fingerprint: Option<String>,
    pub(crate) generation: u64,
    /// Ids the caller minted for this run, consumed in order by `new`.
    pub new_ids: VecDeque<String>,
    entropy: u64,
    minter: crate::ids::Mint,
    /// Every id consumed by this execution, including newly minted ids.
    pub(crate) minted_ids: Vec<String>,
    /// The server's live registry behind `connected[p]`, when there is one.
    pub presence: Option<std::sync::Arc<dyn Presence>>,
}


impl Context {
    pub fn new(viewer: impl Into<String>, args: BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Self {
        Self { #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] refusal_trace: None, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] search_retained_order: None, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] search_retained_candidates: Vec::new(), retained_search: false, read_spent: None, viewer: viewer.into(), kind: "user".into(), args, now: None, read_time: None, row_provenance: false, session_digest: None, wire_args: None, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] delivery_fingerprint: None, generation: 0, new_ids: VecDeque::new(), entropy: 0, minter: crate::ids::Mint::default(), minted_ids: Vec::new(), presence: None }
    }

    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub fn with_session(mut self, token: &str) -> Self {
        self.session_digest = Some(crate::identity::digest(token));
        self
    }

    /// Developer host instrumentation; never accepted from wire arguments.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub fn with_refusal_trace(mut self, trace: refusal_trace::RefusalTrace) -> Self {
        self.refusal_trace = Some(trace); self
    }

    /// Request the metered, output-only source-identity envelope for a query.
    /// A query and the store cut its transaction read, which a refusal also
    /// carries, so a client can order it against answers (LLP 3000.018 §3.2).
    pub fn query_at(&mut self, name: &str, context: Context) -> (Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal>, Option<u64>) {
        let mut cut = None;
        let result = self.query_reading(name, context, &mut cut, None, &mut None, DeliveryManifest::Build, true);
        (result, cut)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.028#3.3 — point overlap and page share one transaction/ledger.
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub fn query_search_at(&mut self, name: &str, context: Context, retained: Option<&[String]>) -> (Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal>, Option<u64>, Option<Vec<String>>,
    ) {
        self.query_search_with_delivery_at(name, context, retained, DeliveryManifest::Build)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.6 — request-local selection, never a reader-wide toggle.
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub fn query_search_with_delivery_at(&mut self, name: &str, context: Context, retained: Option<&[String]>, manifest: DeliveryManifest) -> (Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal>, Option<u64>, Option<Vec<String>>,
    ) {
        let (mut cut, mut current) = (None, None);
        let result = self.query_reading(name, context, &mut cut, retained, &mut current, manifest, true);
        (result, cut, current)
    }

    /// Host evaluation after its one ordinary opening prelude. Retrying this
    /// snapshot never advances an unrelated row from the global opening ledger.
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.003#10 — memory and detached hosts share the same request-local publication scheduling.
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub fn query_materialized_search_with_delivery_at(&mut self, name: &str, context: Context, retained: Option<&[String]>, manifest: DeliveryManifest)
        -> (Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal>, Option<u64>, Option<Vec<String>>) {
        let (mut cut, mut current) = (None, None);
        let result = self.query_reading(name, context, &mut cut, retained, &mut current, manifest, false);
        (result, cut, current)
    }

    fn query_reading(&mut self, name: &str, mut context: Context, cut: &mut Option<u64>, _retained: Option<&[String]>, _current: &mut Option<Vec<String>>, manifest: DeliveryManifest, advance_opening: bool) -> Result<QueryOutcome, Refusal> {
        context.generation = self.generation;
        let requested = context.now.unwrap_or_else(crate::now_ms);
        context.now = Some(if advance_opening { self.advance_time_audience(requested)? } else { requested });
        let program = self.programs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::UnknownOp(name.to_owned()))?.clone();
        if program.kind != ProgramKind::Query {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is a {}, not a query", program.kind.word())).into());
        }
        server_query_allowed(&self.schema, name)?;
        validate::validate_inputs(&self.schema, &program, &mut context.args)?;
        if context.presence.is_none() {
            context.presence = self.presence.clone();
        }
        let tx = self.store.read_transaction()?;
        context.now = Some(time_audience::observe_reader_time(
            &self.schema,
            &tx,
            context.now.unwrap_or(0),
        )?);
        let seq = tx.next_seq()? - 1;
        *cut = Some(seq);
        let touches = self.schema.operations.get(name).is_some_and(|f| !f.ephemeral_reads.is_empty());
        let mut hub = if touches { Some(self.ephemeral.lock()?) } else { None };
        if let Some(hub) = &mut hub {
            hub.bind(&tx.meta("store_id")?.unwrap_or_default(), self.generation, &self.schema)?;
        }
        let version = hub.as_ref().map(|h| h.version());
        let layered = hub.as_ref().map(|h| ephemeral::Layered::new(&tx, &h.rows, &self.schema, false));
        let rows: &dyn crate::rows::Rows = layered.as_ref().map_or(&tx as &dyn crate::rows::Rows, |l| l);
        if program.native {
            // A native read (LLP 3000.005 §9): the module over this viewer's
            // rules under the read meter; the same call a device makes over
            // its held rows.
            #[cfg(feature = "native")]
            {
                let module = self.native.as_deref().ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Native { code: "E_NATIVE".into(), message: format!("{name} is a native read but no module is installed; `snapback4 check` builds it") })?;
                let args = context.args.clone();
                let now = context.now.unwrap_or(0);
                let runner = Runner::new(rows, &self.schema, context, Meter::new(self.ceilings, Cut::Cap), seq);
                let (value, mut runner) = native::run_read(module, runner, name, &args, now);
                let data = match value {
                    Ok(value) => value,
                    Err(RuntimeError::TimeAudiencePending) => {
                        runner.query_unavailable = true;
                        Value::Null
                    }
### 3.4 P8: residency you can see, and a CLI that does not fight the dev server

**Change.**

- `client.residency()` returns rows and bytes per table on the device, with
  the store's total bytes and, on the web, the wasm heap. The guide's residency
  card shows it next to the sync windows that bound it.
- `snapback4 run <job>` and the other store-writing CLI commands detect a live
  dev server on the same store (its lifetime lock file) and either run the work
  through that server or refuse with `E_STORE_BUSY`, naming the server's
  address, instead of failing with `database is locked`. Which of the two is
  the lane's first finding.

**Evidence.** Residency on a fixture with known rows; the CLI against a running
dev server, both ways.

**2026-10-08 — `run` goes through a live dev host (app-farm round 1).** The
lane's first finding was the refusal; 27 of ~270 app-farm builds (41 diaries)
then stopped their dev server to run one operation, or reverse-engineered the
wire. `run` now takes the other branch of this item when the owner is a
responsive `snapback4 dev` (it answers `GET /dev/status`): a query or mutation
goes to `POST /q|/m/<op>` with the persona header naming the same principal a
local run would use, and a job to the dev-only `POST /dev/jobs/<job>`, which
queues it behind earlier runs and drives chunks on the dev clock. No second
writer opens the store. `--now`, a non-persona principal, `serve` and a stopped
host still refuse `E_STORE_BUSY`, now with the equivalent `curl`. Other
store-writing commands keep the refusal.

**2026-09-27 — Darwin ownership correction (lane OW).** Ownership now locks
`<database filename>-owner` beside the canonical database, a regular 0600 file
opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`: exclusive for backend writers, shared for devices
including the native C ABI. On macOS/iOS, a flock on the database itself
conflicts with SQLite's POSIX locks, preventing startup adoption and device
commits. Database identity remains `(dev, ino)`, checked with stat alone;
only a missing database is created and closed before SQLite opens it. The
guard holds the sidecar, never a database descriptor whose close could discard
SQLite's locks. Sidecars persist after unlock and move with the database's
other sidecars; symlinks, hard links and nonregular ownership files are refused.
`serve.lock` remains an address hint, and `E_STORE_BUSY` keeps its meaning.

**2026-09-27 — round-1 file-mutation hardening (lane OW).** Both ownership
and address-file opens lstat the database and candidate before opening and
compare the opened inode again before any lock, read, write or chmod. A
post-open alias or uncertain descriptor is deliberately forgotten, never
closed or unlocked: either operation could discard SQLite's POSIX locks.
Ownership sidecars must be empty, and address records at most 128 bytes;
these stat-only bounds also refuse a SQLite database renamed to either name
when its original path is absent. Lock the sidecar before creating a missing
database; an `E_STORE_BUSY` probe creates no database or address record.
Database hard links are refused with `E_STORE` and “copy the store, do not
hard-link it,” including on an existing owner's path check.

**2026-09-27 — round-2 exclusion boundary (lane OW, option b).** Exclusion
holds while `<database>-owner` is left in place. Deleting or replacing it while
a process runs ends exclusion: a second owner can create and lock a new sidecar
while the first can continue reading and writing. Removal does not revoke a
running process or cancel its transactions. The retained descriptor still locks
the old inode, which no longer coordinates new opens. Stop all owners before
moving a store and move its sidecars with it; leave `-owner` in place for the
whole ownership lifetime.

This replaces round 1's partial fail-closed claim and removes its live sidecar
checks. `Store::conn()` exposes a raw SQLite connection: auth autocommit writes,
auth and worker transactions, and effect/action/job/timeline reads bypass the
store's transaction helpers. There is no single existing fallible interception
point; changing that accessor's return type would change the connection API and
its callers, and an accessor check cannot revoke a previously borrowed
connection. No single-digit-microsecond transaction-cost claim is made. The
database inode, hard-link and acquisition-time sidecar checks remain. Unit,
native-device and two-process regressions label unlink/replacement as this
documented boundary and prove a second owner can start while old work continues.

**2026-09-27 — live token mutual proof (lane TK, round 1).** A busy
`token` command asks the announced owner to mint through its existing writer.
The per-run 0600 `<canonical database>-token` secret never crosses HTTP:
BSD wildcard listeners need not own the specific loopback address the CLI
dials. Both rounds require an actual loopback socket peer, one literal
### 3.6 P10: adding a view never stalls reads

**AP follow-up, 2026-10-04.** Calibration 30 exposed the remaining writer cost
on a different real schema delta: nine indexes, three historical captures and
seven public views. Server activation orders admitted index keys before insertion,
uses a configurable combined main/temp cache target (default 64 MiB, half each),
and reads ahead at most 32 serialized
rows / 128 KiB for its single-row maintained-count scans. The latter keeps every
per-row meter charge and recount boundary; device execution is unchanged.
Count assessment shares one source validation when a whole-source row count
proves every group fits, while sums retain wide accumulation. Sparse projection
and page admission avoid unnecessary copies/decodes. These are changes inside
the same transaction, not online adoption. `N_ADOPTION_PAUSE` exposes source
counts and modeled “up to” work at measured eb1000 rates before the lock; the
subtotal is not a floor and neither number is a latency bound. Retirement-only
and mixed changes count removed/rebuilt predecessor rows and index entries once
per catalog name and explicitly warn of the additional unestimated pause.
Round 2 includes surviving timeline-owned names, dependent aggregate targets,
row-view recount/basis rows and replaced indexes. Tables selected by
`Retirement::affects` (read-through changes or inherited-expiry field/default
changes) warn about retirement/recapture: the deletion subset depends on
activation time, so their stored populations are not exact deletion counts.
On a schema change, an unaffected read-through table separately rewrites every
stored row, including expired rows, to recompute deadlines. These deadline
rewrites are counted exactly; they are not a deletion subset. Their pause
remains unestimated. Maintained-column mirror passes, cap occupancy work and
asset-home ledger retirement also name unestimated work. These populations can
overlap modeled source visits. Legacy live-sum and cap-occupancy repairs remain
visible with no schema delta; preview performs read-only counts, never retirement or row rewrites. Advice also names `SNAPBACK4_ADOPTION_CACHE_MIB`
(0 preserves the caller setting) and the approximately 147 MiB RSS measured with
the round-0 default; cache size is not a process cap. Linux reduces the target
for finite address-space headroom. A borrowed-cache SQLite
`NOMEM` rolls back fully and retries once at the caller setting; publication is
still one atomic commit. Completed adoption prints disjoint phases with nested
index/capture/view attribution, including failed-attempt time when retried. The
[AP report](../snapback4/reports/20261004-ap-adoption-pause/REPORT.md) carries the
new before/after and equivalence evidence; the historical measurements below
remain measurements of their original fixtures.

Calibration #22's M8 read waited 74,978 ms while adoption held the serving
engine. A reproducible engine fixture at the reported source populations has
1,447,960 rows and 8,863,718 index entries. Before optimization, on the lane's
Linux host, test-profile adoption took 171,946 ms and its waiting read took
171,948 ms. Unrelated row projection and index replacement took 142,046 ms;
validation 12,850 ms; commit/checkpoint 6,776 ms. The six new grouped folds
already group in memory and write one row per final key: together 671 ms.

    pub(crate) fn exclude_ephemeral(&mut self, schema: &Schema) {
        self.catalog.retain(|name,_| {
            !schema.tables.iter().any(|(table,t)| {
                t.ephemeral.is_some() && (name == table || name.starts_with(&format!("{table}.")))
            })
        });
    }
    pub fn meta(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        Ok(self.conn.prepare_cached("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1")?.query_row([key], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?)
    }

    pub fn set_meta(&self, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.check_ownership()?;
        self.conn.execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key, value) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value", params![key, value])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub fn conn(&self) -> &Connection {
        &self.conn
    }

struct Kdf<'a>(&'a AtomicUsize);
impl Drop for Kdf<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.0.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::Release);
    }
}
fn limited(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    let mut refusal = Refusal::new(
        "E_RATE_LIMIT",
        "auth",
        "too many identity requests; retry later",
    )
    .guide("personas");
    refusal.retryable = true;
    respond_timed(request, shared, 429, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal) }));
}

pub fn handle(request: Request, shared: &Shared, path: &str, body: &str) {
    let payload: Json = match serde_json::from_str(if body.is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(value) => value,
        Err(_) => {
            return respond_timed(request, shared,
                400,
                json!({ "denied": Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "identity takes a JSON object") }),
            );
        }
    };
    let email = payload.get("email").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    let password = payload.get("password").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    let password_route = matches!(path, "/auth/login" | "/auth/signup");
    let peer = request
        .remote_addr()
        .map(|a| a.ip().to_string())
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "unknown".into());
    let rate_route = matches!(path, "/auth/login" | "/auth/signup" | "/auth/guest");
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — dev loopback has no minute counters; KDF slots remain
    let dev_loopback = shared.personas && request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|peer| peer.ip().is_loopback());
    if rate_route && !dev_loopback
        && !shared
            .auth
            .attempt(&peer, password_route.then_some(email), Instant::now())
    {
        return limited(request, shared);
    }
    let kdf = if password_route {
        match shared.auth.kdf(dev_loopback) {
            Some(permit) => Some(permit),
            None => return limited(request, shared),
        }
    } else {
        None
    };
    let scope = match payload.get("scope") {
        None => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Full),
        Some(Json::String(value)) if value == "full" => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Full),
        Some(Json::String(value)) if value == "read" => Ok(identity::SessionScope::Read),
        _ => Err(RuntimeError::Input("scope must be full or read".into())),
    };
    let outcome = scope.and_then(|scope| perform(&request, shared, path, email, password, scope));
    drop(kdf);
    match outcome {
        Ok(Outcome::Refused(why)) => respond_refused(request, shared, why),
        Ok(Outcome::SupervisedRefresh) => respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new(
            "E_WORKER_REFRESH", "auth", "supervised worker tokens are rotated by the supervisor; keep using the injected token",
        ).guide("effects")) })),
        Ok(Outcome::Logout) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "ok": true })),
        Ok(Outcome::LogoutAll(revoked)) => {
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "ok": true, "revoked": revoked }))
        }
        Ok(Outcome::Session(session)) => {
            let mut wire = json!({ "principal": session.principal, "kind": session.kind, "token": session.token, "expiresAt": session.expires_at, "scope": session.scope });
            if let Some(cap) = session.cap_expires_at { wire["capExpiresAt"] = json!(cap); }
            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "session": wire }));
        },
        Err(error) => {
            if matches!(path, "/auth/logout" | "/auth/narrow" | "/auth/refresh" | "/auth/logout-all")
                && let RuntimeError::Auth(message) = &error {
                return respond_refused(request, shared, Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", message).guide("personas"));

The new race tests cover privileged writes but not this case. 1. **[P2] Persona read authority can survive password-only adoption and execute against the new schema.**  
   [serve_identity.rs:40](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_identity.rs:40) checks the advertised door policy and returns an unversioned persona capability. The query handler authenticates at [serve.rs:1137](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:1137), then separately acquires its reader at [serve.rs:1257](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:1257), with no persona-policy revalidation before execution.

   A concrete interleaving is: authenticate a declared persona while doors are open; pause before reader acquisition; adopt password-only identity; resume using the replacement reader. The query executes the new generation as `dev:<persona>` despite its closed door policy. It can therefore invoke a newly introduced query or obtain its newly protected result without password authentication.

   **Repair:** bind persona authentication—including door availability and the declared cast—to the generation/snapshot used for execution, rejecting or reauthenticating when it changes. Add a deterministic barrier regression between authentication and reader acquisition. The new [serve_stage tests:35](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/serve_stage/mod.rs:35) cover queued privileged writes, but omit this read path. This is an implementation defect, not missing future qualification.
